When the regret window closes clean, `confirmed` goes up by one: at 1 the phrasing serves exact repeats permanently; at `learning.phrasing_paraphrase_uses` (2) it also serves L2 paraphrases through the embedding model, still only for slot-free targets, and only under two guards: the cosine similarity between the new utterance and the phrasing is at least `learning.phrasing_min_similarity` (0.85 until WP7 measures it on the resolver golden file's paraphrases and records the value that keeps every negative case out), and every app, file, media, URL or monitor name that the L1 slot resolvers find in the new utterance equals the matching literal in the target's arguments (or the alias's value). A paraphrase that fails either guard is only a hint to the brain. So "bring Word up" never runs the confirmed "get Notepad up for me", however close their embeddings, because the `App` resolver finds Word and the target launches Notepad. Without an adopted transformer model, L2 uses the static-embedding model (section 3.3.10), so paraphrase matching never depends on a model that might not be adopted.

**The episodic ledger** records Eileen's own events plus sampled focus changes (app, window title, document identity, focus time and, for a browser window, its address-bar URL after redaction, which rule 3 of section 2.12's URL rule reads) for 14 days and answers "go back to what we tried an hour ago", "what was that page I had open yesterday" and the Photoshop recall in E8; InPrivate and incognito windows, password managers and private apps are never recorded, and off-the-record mode records nothing. Ledger lines go into a brain turn only when the utterance refers to the past, or when the brain calls `ledger_search`. At most about 600 tokens per brain turn come from learning.

**12. Privacy of what is stored.** `learn.sqlite` and `eileen.db` run with `PRAGMA secure_delete=ON`; after a forget, each database's WAL is checkpointed with `TRUNCATE` and blobs are reference-counted, so unreferenced ones are deleted. A forget is one plan from `eileen_skills::forget::plan`, listing the skill versions, facts, phrasings (every phrasing whose source turn or source `Act` job is in the plan, because a phrasing stores the user's own utterance), the answers-cache rows and source-statistics topics those jobs alone wrote, evidence jobs and their turns, carried out by two owners: the core emits `Effect::Learn(LearnCmd::Forget(plan))`, which `eileen-learn` (WP7) executes over `learn.sqlite`, the blobs, incidents, recordings, saved scenarios and snapshot folders, and `Effect::Persist(Record::PurgeJobs { jobs, turns })`, which `eileen-runtime::store` (WP2) executes over `eileen.db` (the jobs' rows in `jobs`, `events`, `approvals` and `journal`, and every `events` row carrying one of those turns). With `logging.include_personal` on, the runtime also rewrites that day's and older log files without the lines that carry those job or turn IDs. The forget reports done only when both `LearnEvent::Forgot` and `StoreEvent::Purged` have arrived (and any live snapshot folder holding the skill has been deleted, item 3). Notes are in the plan too (D77, item 15): `ForgetPlan.notes` lists each note with its source turn, the `CallId`s of the calls that wrote it and the turns whose `[notes]` block or bind turn carried it, so the plan removes the topic file (overwritten before it is deleted) and its index line, any held copy, its index row, receipts that cite only it, those steps, hook rows and turns, and every recording that holds them; it writes a tombstone of keyed hashes of the note's subject key and of its eight-token shingles, never plaintext, which the guard reads to deny a later write that restates it. The acceptance test greps the whole data directory for a canary request string afterwards. The canary request is a slot-free paraphrase the brain runs through its `act` tools (so its `Act` job creates a phrasing candidate holding the canary words), said twice so the second time runs as an exact repeat, and also asked once as a deep-answer question (so it reaches the answers cache and a trajectory); then each of those jobs is forgotten (`learned_forget` with `Job { job }`). Two more cases cover notes: a canary note written by the fact path and one written by a stub coding worker, each then forgotten with "forget that I ...". The grep decompresses every `.zst` blob and zstd frame, dumps every text column of every SQLite file and reads each WAL file raw, reads every log, and runs twice, with `logging.include_personal` off and on. At promotion, evidence shrinks to what replay and measurement need (slot values, tool names, bound arguments, checks, cost), dropping free-text results. The learning folder gets a user-only ACL and is marked not to be indexed by Windows Search. Encryption at rest is v0.2 (decision D40).

**13. Measurement, and skills as tests.** Per skill: uses, successes, failures, undos, handoffs, and median duration, model turns and tokens of the first runs against the last ten; "time saved" is the difference times the uses.

**Receipts** (idea 1 of section 1's second proposed-ideas table; D77). Every addressed request ends with a `Receipt` the core builds (section 3.3.13): the layer that served it (L1, L2, the brain, a replay or a worker), the learned records it cited (phrasing ids, the skill id and version, the notes and their hashes), the vendor memory in context (the notes folder, the hash of the index the session started with, the topic files opened and the notes written, from the `PostToolUse` hook, and the instruction files `InstructionsLoaded` reported), the route and resolved model id, and the model turns, tokens and time from the `result` event. `Resolution` and `ToolSource` name the exact record (section 3.3.10, with the phrasing id that section 3.3.13's provisional shapes add to `ToolSource`). "Why did you do that?" reads the receipt back; "don't do that again" retracts the records it cites after one confirmation (a note is deleted with a tombstone, a phrasing is deleted, a skill is demoted and a repair queued). Receipts are stored in `learn.sqlite` for 30 days, are part of forget plans, and feed a weekly roll-up, beside the per-skill savings, of the share of requests that needed no model. Golden tests check that each receipt names exactly its records, and that B8-curve's receipts alone reproduce its run-by-run table.

When a skill becomes trusted, Eileen writes local sim-lane scenarios from its evidence, and every failed job and every replay handoff becomes a local scenario automatically, with `outcome = "expected_fail"` and its incident attached (owner direction: trajectories become regression scenarios automatically). These stay in `scenarios\saved\`, capped at 200 and 500 MB with the oldest auto-generated ones removed first, and are never shared without the share flow. A librarian repair is committed only if the skill's scenarios still replay green. Once per trusted skill, the HUD offers "Save this as a test for the project?" through the normal share preview and redaction. Fleet results carry anonymous learning statistics (skills per level, handoff rate, median turns by run index; no skill names or text). Sharing skills across many machines is v0.3; the fleet will check a skill on several machines before it can ever be offered.

**14. The worked recipes, with real tool names.**

Pointers are written `$step <id> <pointer>`, slots `$slot <name>` and lengths `$len $step <id> <pointer>`, short for the JSON forms above; `post` checks the step's own output unless it names a check op. `call <tool> <op> {args}` names the op the same way for every tool, including those whose argument struct selects it through an `action` field (`apps_media status` is `AppsMedia { action: Status }`; section 3.3.9, "Ops and selectors").

`resolve-sharpen-face` (slots `t: Offset`, `which: Position`, `amount: Choice`, and the asked slot `project: Pick`), on the Fusion route:

1. `hello`: `call apps_resolve hello` (post: `/ok` is true).
2. `info`: `call apps_resolve timeline_info` (post: `/timeline` exists). Fallback when no timeline is current: `recent`: `call apps_resolve projects_recent` (post: `/projects` count at least 1); `which_project`: `ask` "Which project?" with options from `$step recent /projects`, into the asked slot `project` (a pointer to an earlier step of the same fallback, which the scope rule allows); `load`: `call apps_resolve load_project {name: $slot project}` (post: `/loaded` is true); then `info` runs again, and a second failure hands off.
3. `page`: `call apps_resolve open_page {page: "edit"}` (post: `/page` equals `"edit"`); `goto`: `call apps_resolve goto {offset_s: $slot t}` (post: check `apps_resolve timeline_info`, `/playhead_frame` within 1 of `$step goto /target_frame`, the frame Rust computed from the timeline's start and rate).
4. `still`: `call apps_resolve export_still` (post: `/path` exists); `faces`: `call apps_image faces {path: $step still /path}` (post: `/faces` count at least 1); `pick`: `call apps_image pick_face {path: $step still /path, which: $slot which}` (post: `/rect` exists); `before`: `call apps_image sharpness {path: $step still /path, rect: $step pick /rect}` (post: `/sharpness` exists), the baseline.
5. `confirm` "This face, on the right?" showing `$step pick /crop` (implicit postcondition: the user's yes).
6. `clip`: `call apps_resolve current_item` (post: `/end_offset_s` exists); `stills`: `call apps_resolve export_stills {from_offset_s: $step clip /start_offset_s, to_offset_s: $step clip /end_offset_s}` (post: `/stills` count at least 2); `track`: `call apps_image track_face {stills: $step stills /stills, start: $step pick /rect}` (post: `/boxes` count at least and at most `$len $step stills /stills`).
7. `edit`: `call apps_resolve import_fusion_comp {template: "sharpen_ellipse", boxes: $step track /boxes, amount: $slot amount}` (post: check `apps_resolve fusion_comps`, `/current` equals `$step edit /name`). The dispatcher journals its inverse (delete this composition, load the previous one). On the DRX route this step is two steps: `addv`: `call apps_resolve add_version` (post: check `apps_resolve versions`, `/current` equals `$step addv /name`), then `edit`: `call apps_resolve apply_drx {template: "sharpen_window", window: $step pick /rect, all_layers: true}` (post: `/applied` is true). On the computer-use route it is `edit`: `delegate` "apply a sharpen window at this box on the Color page" with inputs `{box: $step pick /rect}` (post: `/outcome` equals `"done"`); the delegate's own check is only the worker's report, and the objective check is step 8's sharpness postcondition, which runs next as an ordinary `call` (a failure there hands off). This keeps the skill at level 3 with one delegated step.
8. `after_still`: `call apps_resolve export_still` (post: `/path` exists); `after`: `call apps_image sharpness {path: $step after_still /path, rect: $step pick /rect}` (post: `/sharpness` at least 1.15 times `$step before /sharpness`).

Every non-read argument is a slot, a pointer or a vocabulary literal (`"edit"`, `"sharpen_ellipse"`, `"sharpen_window"`, `true`, `"done"`), every `call` and `delegate` step has a postcondition, and every check calls only read-only tier-0 ops (`fusion_comps`, `versions`, `timeline_info`), so the recipe is eligible, and on the Fusion route it is model-free from end to end.

`youtube-play-at` (slots `video: Media`, `t: Offset`), for phrasings the built-in grammar does not cover:

1. `match`: `call apps_recent {query: $slot video, kinds: ["media"]}` (post: `/confident` is true). Fallback: `find`: `delegate` "find this video on YouTube and play it" with inputs `{video: $slot video}` (post: `/outcome` equals `"done"`, and check `apps_media status`, `/title` exists); the worker's `apps_open_url` call and SMTC put the video into the media history, so when `match` runs again it finds it; a second failure hands off.
2. `open`: `call apps_open_url {url: $step match /best/url, start_s: $slot t, wait_for_media_title: $step match /best/title, reuse_playing: true}` (post: `/media_title_seen` is true). With `reuse_playing`, an SMTC session that already shows that title is sought to `start_s` through SMTC instead of the URL being opened again (`/reused` is true), so after the fallback's worker has already opened and played the video, the recipe does not open it a second time.
3. `pos`: `call apps_media status` (post: `/position_s` within 3 of `$slot t`, read after any ad has passed).

The fallback's delegate goal ("find this video on YouTube and play it", eight tokens the librarian wrote) traces to nothing in the evidence, so the draft holds that one line for the user's approval (item 9) rather than failing the lint; once approved it traces as `Approved`. The computer-use route's goal in `resolve-sharpen-face` is held the same way.

`launch-agents-fanout` (slots `n: Count`, `task: Text`, `repo: File`): `call jobs_dispatch {kind: "coding", fanout: $slot n, mode: "compete", repo: $slot repo, task: $slot task}` (post: `/jobs` count at least and at most `$slot n`, queued ones included). `jobs_dispatch` is allowed from origin `Replay` by item 4, with `authorized_by` taken from the replay job. The coding itself is always a worker; only the orchestration is compiled.

**15. Claude's own notes: where the person lives** (decision D77, from the owner's direction of 2026-10-22; research in [2026-10-learning-the-person.md](research/2026-10-learning-the-person.md)). The claude CLI's auto memory saves `user`, `feedback`, `project` and `reference` notes as Markdown: a `MEMORY.md` index whose first 200 lines or 25 KB load into every session, plus topic files read when needed, written with the ordinary `Read`, `Write` and `Edit` tools ([memory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory#auto-memory)). Eileen builds on those notes instead of keeping a second model of the person: it reads them, shows them, guards their writes, corrects and forgets them, and writes the ones that come from the user's own words itself, in the same format.

- **Where the notes live.** Two kinds of folder, both in Eileen's data directory, so they follow `EILEEN_DATA_DIR` and stay inside the forget plan and the canary grep, with a user-only ACL and out of Windows Search: the hub, `<data_dir>\memory\claude\`, for `user`, `feedback` and `reference` notes about the person, and one folder per repository, `<data_dir>\memory\claude-repos\<repo-hash>\`, for `project` notes, shared by that repository's worktrees as the CLI shares its own per-repository memory. `eileen setup` (and the first `eileen run` in a fresh data directory) creates the hub with an empty `MEMORY.md`, so the brain's prompt file always exists. The hub is written only by Eileen's writer and is never any session's `autoMemoryDirectory`. A session that keeps notes natively (a coding session, in its repository's folder) gets its folder through `autoMemoryDirectory` in its settings file ([settings reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#automemorydirectory)), which works under `--setting-sources ""` (probe on 2.1.304). Before a job is bound, the harness checks that the memory path in `system/init` is that folder; a session whose path differs is stopped and started again with memory off, so a note never lands in the per-repository folder under the user's own Claude folder that the owner's own sessions load. Eileen's code never reads or writes under the user's `.claude` folder (the one proposed exception waits for the owner, "Imports" below).
- **Who reads what** (`MemorySpec`, section 3.3.13). The web and screen workers keep memory off, and the path guard denies them the notes folders (section 2.12), because a page they read could tell them to send the whole index somewhere; the brain keeps it off because its context always holds window titles and a recall side call would sit on the voice path.

| Session | The CLI's auto memory | How it sees the notes |
|---|---|---|
| Brain and its spare | Off | The hub's `MEMORY.md` through `--append-system-prompt-file` at spawn (section 2.3), plus the `[notes]` block each turn |
| Coding | On, writing natively in its repository's folder, once the gates below pass; off until then | Its repository folder's index natively (until then, that folder's notes as lines in its bind turn), and the hub's index read-only through `--append-system-prompt-file`, with the hub's topic files open to `Read` unless the kind keeps `--restricted` (then their bodies come as bind-turn lines, gate 4) |
| `continue_session` | Off, because its transcript persists | None in v0.1: its transcript persists under the user's own Claude folder, where Eileen never reads or deletes, so a note carried into it would escape a forget (and a resumed conversation keeps the system prompt it recorded, so a prompt file would not reach it either). It gets note lines in its bind turn only if WP3's probe shows its fork runs with `--no-session-persistence` |
| Computer use, research, deep answers, general, and the warm pool | Off in v0.1 | Note lines the core picks for the job in its bind turn (`JobBinding.facts`, about 400 tokens, scoped as item 11 says; a deep answer gets its topic's preferences only) |
| Librarian, scribe, and the one-turn alias and note-format probes | Off | None |

So in v0.1 Claude's own note-taking runs only in coding jobs, once the gates below pass (and, for a kind that keeps `--restricted`, as proposals through Eileen's writer); the brain and the other workers read notes but neither write nor propose them, and almost every note about the person comes from Eileen's fact path, written in Claude's format. That narrows the owner's ask of 2026-10-22, so section 7 puts it to him ("Waiting for the owner", question 3), with the alternative of giving the brain and general workers `learned_note_propose`, every proposal held on the HUD for Keep or Drop.

- **The gates before any session writes natively.** (1) WP3's memory probe, on the pinned and the latest CLI with the exact flags of section 2.5: a brain with a poisoned window title, a deep worker on a fixture page and a coding worker each run with memory on, and every change in their memory folders must match, by path and BLAKE3 hash, a `PreToolUse`-approved `Write` or `Edit` or a write by Eileen's writer. The probe also records whether memory extraction runs under `-p` stream-json and writes when `--tools` lacks `Write`, whether recall brings topic notes into context with no `Read`, what recall adds to the brain's time to first text with a 200-line index, which files the CLI creates in the folder, and whether extraction or recall can be turned off alone. (2) WP7's note acceptance (the held write, the forget canary, the real-CLI concurrent-writer test), after which WP7's last note pull request flips `learning.vendor_memory.claude` from `off` to `on`. (3) `--restricted` (section 2.5): a kind for which it works keeps it, and writes natively only if memory also works under it; otherwise its notes go through Eileen's writer (`writes = ViaEileen`, through a `learned_note_propose` call checked by the same guard) until the owner decides. (4) A memory folder never goes into `--add-dir`: that flag carries only the repository root (section 2.5), and with `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD` a file in an added folder could load as an instruction file. So a kind that could write its pinned folder only through `--add-dir` (WP3's probe of a pinned `autoMemoryDirectory` outside the working folders) writes `ViaEileen`, and a kind that keeps `--restricted`, whose file tools reach only its working folders, reads note bodies only as the note lines the core puts in its bind turn. Until the gates hold, every session keeps `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1`, and the notes still exist: Eileen's writer keeps them in the CLI's format, and sessions read them as the table says.
- **Writes and the guard.** The `Notes` path class (section 2.12) covers only the folders a session's `MemorySpec` names: its own repository's folder, and the hub for a session that holds the hub's index (a coding session). Every other path under `memory\` is `EileenData`, tier 3, so a web or screen worker cannot read the hub and a coding job cannot read another repository's notes. Reading a note in its own folders is tier 0 and sets `private_read`, but it is not an observed read and taints nothing, because every note in those folders was written by the user, by Eileen's writer from the user's words or an approval, or by a traced write of an untainted job; a change nobody approved is restored and held as soon as the watcher sees it, and the sessions that loaded that folder since are recycled (below). A worker may `Write` or `Edit` only in its own session's memory folder, only a file name matching the CLI's own note pattern (a lowercase slug ending in `.md`, or `MEMORY.md`), and only when its `MemorySpec` writes natively; instruction-file names (`CLAUDE*.md`, `AGENTS.md` and the other names the CLIs read), subfolders, other file types, other memory folders and any shell command that writes into one are tier 3. With learning stopped, or in a no-learn or off-the-record job, every note write is denied ("Controls" below). Otherwise a permitted note write is tier 1 only when the job is untainted and the note either traces to the user's own words in that job (a contiguous span of, or a run of eight tokens shared with, its authorizing turn and steers) or is a `project` note in that repository's folder that overlaps no file text the job read. The runtime attaches a `NoteFact` to the hook request, as it attaches path and URL facts, so the decision stays pure: whether the added text shares eight tokens with the user's transcripts, and whether it shares eight tokens with any file text the job read (the results of its `Read`, `Grep` and shell read calls anywhere, a README, source files and dependency folders such as `node_modules`, `vendor`, `target` and `.venv` included, plus every instruction file `InstructionsLoaded` reported; the runtime keeps keyed hashes of those results' eight-token shingles for the job's life, never the text). A note that paraphrases what it read shares no eight tokens with it and passes this test; section 7 lists that as an open risk, and the `all` value of `learning.notes_review` (below) is the answer for a user who wants none of it. Every other model-written note, and every note write from a tainted job whatever its sources (the desktop tree included), is held: the guard answers deny with "held for the user's review; do not retry", the runtime saves the proposed file and any index edit naming it in `learning\pending\notes\`, and `HudPanel::NoteReview` shows it as Claude's note with Keep and Drop, never spoken unprompted; on Keep, Eileen's writer writes both. Whatever its provenance, the text a call adds (`Write` content, `Edit` `new_string`) is deny-scanned against item 9's deny patterns, a URL that traces only to a tainted read, and the tombstones; a hit is denied as rejected. `learning.notes_review = "all"` holds every model-written note. The worker prompt tells Claude to save lasting preferences the user states and never text read from pages, files or the screen; the guard backs that up, and the prompt alone is not trusted. Notes are context, not enforcement ([memory](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory#claude-md-vs-auto-memory)): nothing read from a note sets a tier, creates a grant, adds an MCP server, widens `--tools` or vouches a URL, and a note asking for fewer confirmations is rejected by the deny patterns, with the HUD explaining that confirmation preferences are settings (section 2.11).
- **Eileen's writer and the watcher** (`eileen_learn::notes`). The deterministic fact path, `learned_correct`, approved held notes and approved imports become native notes written with no model, in the frontmatter shape the installed CLI writes (recorded per CLI version in `run\note-format.json` by a one-turn probe like the model-alias probe), with an Eileen provenance key. Eileen's index lines carry no personal values (no numbers, dates, contact details, paths or names outside the app index); the values stay in topic files, and reading a topic file marked sensitive sets `private_read` like any note. That rule binds Eileen's own writes and the tidy pass only; Claude's own index edits are not denied for it. Adds append to `MEMORY.md` in place; a removal rewrites the file and then reconciles: Eileen sees every Claude write to `MEMORY.md` in the stream, and after each write checks that every line it expects, its own and those Claude sessions added, is still there, re-adding any that is missing, journaled (a line it removed for a `Stale` retirement, item 11, is no longer expected). Writes retry with backoff on a Windows sharing violation, and a note's bytes are overwritten before its file is deleted. A debounced watcher re-indexes every change. A change that matches an approved `Write` or `Edit` of an Eileen session, or a write by the writer, is indexed with that provenance; any other change, a hand edit included, is unattributed: the writer restores the earlier bytes from its journal, saves the change in `learning\pending\notes\` as "changed outside Eileen" for Keep or Drop, and recycles the sessions that loaded that folder since. `user`, `feedback` and `reference` notes written in a repository folder are moved into the hub, journaled and under the same rules, so the person stays in one place; notes of one type with the same normalised body written by one fan-out group are merged. When the hub's index nears about 120 lines, the HUD offers "tidy my notes".
- **The derived index and the core.** `learn.sqlite` keeps one row per note: the fact row of item 11 with a `NoteRef` (file and BLAKE3 hash), its kind, the subject key and scope for notes Eileen wrote, provenance (`User`, `Worker`, `Imported` or `Approved`), the writing session and job (from `originSessionId`, mapped to the `ws_` id that `--session-id` pins), a sensitive flag and the time last confirmed, rebuilt from the folders at start and on every change, with no note text. The core is sans-IO, so `eileen-learn` pushes the note set (metadata, and each body as blob-backed `Personal` text) with a monotonic `NotesVersion` in `LearnEvent::Notes`, at start and on every change. The harness records the version current just before each spawn and echoes it in `BrainEvent::Ready` and `WorkerEvent::Init`; the core keeps it per instance, not per slot, so the deltas of `[notes]` and of a pooled worker's bind turn are everything after it (a duplicate is harmless and a miss impossible), and a forget recycles exactly the instances whose starting version held the note. Relevance is a pure function in `eileen-skills` (`notes::relevant`). A Claude-written note has no subject key, so a spoken correction or forget matches it by text and asks once ("Claude noted ...; replace that too?").
- **Recording and replay.** `record::stub_blobs` turns every note text into a `{"$blob"}` reference whatever its size (`Notes`-class `Write` content and `Edit` `new_string` in hook inputs and `tool_use` arguments, note bodies in `LearnEvent`s and in `CoreSnapshotV1`); forget deletes those blobs, and the replayer reports a missing one as a redacted input, not a divergence. In scenario mode a note change caused by a worker's tool call is reactive, keyed to that call and served after its `tool_result`; a hand edit is scheduled; Eileen's own writes are synthesized by the replayer's learning-store model, which gains a notes model; the watcher emits a change that matches an in-flight call only after that call's `tool_result`; and a scenario may carry a notes fixture folder copied into the fresh data directory. WP1 and WP7 apply these rules to sections 3.3.14 and 3.3.16 by amendment.
- **Controls.** "What do you know about me?" answers in two sentences and lists on the HUD, grouped by where each item lives: Claude's notes on this PC (kind, who wrote it: you said it, Claude noted it, imported or approved, and when); after v0.1 Codex's and Grok's notes, read-only and labelled with the vendor; Eileen's own items (names for things, dictation words, sources you liked, the last 14 days of activity, the voice profile, settings); and a fixed line that memories kept by the vendors' apps live on their servers, are not visible to Eileen, and are managed in each app's settings. "Show me what Claude noted today" lists new notes with Undo for 30 s; `eileen notes list|show|forget|keep|drop` does the same from a terminal; a HUD button opens the folder, whose files are plain Markdown. A spoken correction ("no, my timelines start at zero", or "that's wrong" about a note) retires the notes with the same subject key in the same scope or none, writes the user's version, journaled so "undo that" restores the files, and queues repairs of the skills whose `facts_used` lists the old note. "Forget that I like jazz" finds the notes through the derived index (subject key first, then text), asks once and runs item 12's plan; forgetting a job removes the notes it wrote. "Tidy my notes" lists notes retired as `Stale` (unconfirmed for 90 days, item 11) and exact duplicates and removes them after a yes, with no model; a model-run tidy waits for the question of a native consolidation pass (section 7). "Don't learn from this", the first-run demo and off the record deny every note write for the job ("the user asked not to learn from this job") while sessions still read the hub, and the writer stores nothing; "stop learning" stops all note writes and keeps reading; `learning.vendor_memory.claude = off` keeps the CLI's auto memory off in every session.
- **Consent and settings.** The first-run privacy note gains one line and a toggle, on by default once the default flips: "Claude keeps notes on this PC about how you like things done. You can see, change or delete any of them." `learning.vendor_memory.<route>`, `learning.notes_review`, `learning.person_view.<route>`, `learning.import_claude_notes`, `learning.use_claude_instructions` and `privacy.vendor_cloud.<route>` are Safety keys (section 2.11), each asked once and stored with the approval that made it.
- **Imports (proposed, pending the owner).** With his yes to D77's narrowing of D4, setup and a weekly idle check would offer, line by line, the `user` and `feedback` notes his own Claude sessions keep under `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\projects\*\memory\`, as copies with provenance `Imported` and a hash of the source path, the way Codex's importer copies Claude project memories ([import](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/import.md)); and his `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.claude/rules/*.md` could reach sessions through `--append-system-prompt-file`, never through the user setting source, which would bring his allow rules and hooks into workers. Those files would be deny-scanned at every spawn, asked about again with the diff when a hash changes after the yes, placed after Eileen's own rules inside a fence that says they cannot grant permissions or change confirmations, copied into `run\<session>\` and deleted when the session exits. Only `eileen_learn::vendor_notes` would read them, through section 2.12's canonicalisation: no reparse point followed, and a final path outside the allowlist, a file with more than one hard link or one over a size cap refused with nothing read; never a credential file, `settings.json`, `~/.claude.json` or a transcript. Until he answers, none of this is built and nothing reads under his `.claude` folder.
- **Cloud-side memories: local first.** The design uses only vendor memory stored on the PC; that is the recommendation put to the owner, and it applies unless he says otherwise. `privacy.vendor_cloud.<route>` is off by default; when the owner turns one on, the only path is that vendor's own manual bridge (Claude's memory export and "Start import", [import and export](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12123587-import-and-export-your-memory-from-claude); ChatGPT's "ask what it remembers"), with pasted text treated as untrusted and approved line by line before it becomes a note. Eileen never signs in to a vendor app or drives one with computer use to read memory. claude.ai synced skills, Codex cloud tasks and Codex's importer of Claude memory count as cloud or cross-vendor and stay off; the importer is the owner's own act only. D77 states the alternative, cloud memories as first-class sources, with its costs.
- **Other routes, after v0.1.** No user-level location is read by every vendor, so one derived file, `<data_dir>\memory\person.md` (an AGENTS.md-style rendering of the `user` and `feedback` notes the user lets travel, regenerated on every change and never edited on its own, where each line can be marked "keep this one local"), reaches each route through its own per-session channel after a per-route yes (`learning.person_view.<route>`), and person lines another route learns natively are offered once for promotion into the hub. *Codex*: the owner's own `CODEX_HOME` and sign-in; memory set only through per-thread or per-process overrides, never the global config; worker threads start with memory off and turn it on only for kinds that read nothing from outside (or run with `memories.disable_on_external_context` per process); brain turns, probes and speculative turns are ephemeral; Eileen's framing and `person.md` go in `developerInstructions`, which Codex keeps out of memory input; "what do you know about me" shows its User Profile and User preferences sections read-only; `MemoryCitation` entries join receipts; `memory/reset` is offered only with a confirmation that names both losses (the owner's own Codex memories, and Computer History where it exists); there is no forget by sentence until it is verified; learned skills go through `skills/extraRoots/set`; `approvals_reviewer` stays `user`. *Grok*: every Claude compatibility switch is turned off by environment for each child, with no config edit; `GROK_MEMORY=0` for every worker except kinds that read nothing from outside; `person.md` in `_meta.rules`; the memory files read for inspection and edited only after a yes; Grok's credential file in the `Credential` class. *DeepSeek*: its own notes folder, `<data_dir>\memory\deepseek\`, unless `learning.vendor_memory.deepseek = shared` after a yes, because sharing sends the notes to DeepSeek; notes and `person.md` first in the prompt and changed in batches, so its prefix cache stays warm. *Local models*: a local brain gets `person.md` as its system message; a local model under the claude CLI shares the hub only when its endpoint is loopback and its model tag is not a cloud tag, and is otherwise a cloud route with its own yes. Eileen installs no user-scope Claude hooks or rules, because Grok would run them too.
- **Other native strengths.** `eileen doctor` and the nightly drift job require claude CLI 2.1.284 or later, the release that neutralises invisible characters and markup imitation in notes ([changelog](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md)), and check the note frontmatter shape and `autoMemoryDirectory` behaviour. In the owner's nightly lane and within the librarian's cap, each level 1 or 2 text skill gets `claude plugin eval` cases built from its evidence, run against a no-plugin baseline ([plugin evals](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugin-evals)). After v0.1, each behind a WP3 probe: coding jobs persisted and resumed with a pinned `--session-id` once one session's transcript can be deleted for a forget; an output style for the narration rules; plan mode for "show me first"; subagents once the guard is shown to see their calls; rewind over stream-json; prompt suggestions for the HUD's suggestions.
- **Benchmarks.** B8, B8-curve and WP7 acceptance 7 start with an empty hub and run with `learning.vendor_memory.claude = off` for the skill measurement; a separate variant runs with notes on and records the notes written per run in the receipts, so the two effects are measured apart.

### 2.11 Settings by voice, and configuration

**Files.** TOML with comments (`toml` 1.1.8), in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen\config\`: `eileen.toml` (the user's settings, every key documented in a comment), `profiles\<name>.toml` (overlays), and a generated `eileen.schema.json` for editor completion. The commented default file and the schema are generated from the config types' doc comments by `cargo xtask codegen` and checked fresh by a test. `eileen config explain` prints every effective value and where it came from (default, file, profile, voice, HUD).

**The settings model** (`eileen-config`, section 3.3.11). Every setting has a `SettingSpec`: key, type, default, doc, whether it is reachable by voice and on the HUD (all are, unless stated), its risk class, how it applies (on the next turn, live, or restart of one component) and its safety bound. A test fails unless every leaf key of the config schema has exactly one `SettingSpec` with a matching doc, and the reverse. Changes go through one flow, whatever the source:

1. **Propose.** The brain calls `settings_propose` with the changes and the user's words, the L1 grammar does it for fixed phrasings, or the HUD's settings panel does it for a click. The proposal is validated against the schema and the safety bounds. The utterance the brain passes is informational only and is never evidence of consent.
2. **Check before applying** when a choice could misfire. A new name: not on a common-word list, not close to a household member's name in the user model, and recognised in three of three sentences Eileen synthesizes and transcribes with its own recognizer. A new hotkey: a trial `RegisterHotKey` that fails if another app owns it (and approve and deny chords need at least two modifiers). A new voice or a new engine for a voice: the real-time factor of one clause in that engine, which decides whether it may speak live. A new brain or deep backend: the brain benchmark of section 2.3 against the current backend, with a warning when it is noticeably slower or less reliable. A fitted setting of section 2.16 given a value instead of `auto`: the plan computed with it (`MachineFit`, evaluated in the core with no IO), with its cost said and a question first when it does not fit the PC's usual memory.
3. **Confirm** in one sentence. Low-risk changes apply at once with "say undo that to go back"; changes with a warning ask first.
4. **Apply and version.** Every applied change writes a row in `settings_versions` (full snapshot, diff, source, the utterance); "undo that" or "go back to how it was" applies an earlier snapshot as a new version, so history only grows. Hand edits of the file are picked up by a watcher, validated (an invalid file keeps the previous version and the HUD names the line), and versioned with source `file`. Comments survive every apply.
5. **Profiles** ("work mode", "editing mode") are overlays switched by voice and exportable as one TOML file.

**Safety bounds.** Tier 3 can never be relaxed. A relaxation for a tier-1 action is allowed by voice. Skipping confirmation for a tier-2 action needs an explicit, logged decision: Eileen names the action, the user says "yes, always allow <action>" (or clicks it on the HUD), and the decision is stored with the approval that made it; it can never cover credentials, payments, sending messages as the user or deleting outside a repository. **Safety-relevant keys** (risk `Safety`) change only with an approval built from the user's own voice or click, never because the brain proposed it: every `desktop.*` key (including `desktop.visible_intent_ms` and `desktop.lease_idle_s`), every `privacy.*` key, `logging.include_personal`, `permissions.*` (private apps, desktop apps, grants, `uri_schemes`), the approvals rows of `announcements`, `approvals.*`, every `fleet.*` key (`opt_in`, `mode`, `upload`, `schedule`), `recording.audio`, `learning.distill`, `mcp.servers`, every `hotkeys.*` key, `conversation.window_s`, `routing.brain`, `routing.deep`, `routing.fallback_brain` and every per-kind `route` (another backend sends data to another vendor), `claude_plugin.share_skills`, and, from D77, every `learning.vendor_memory.*` and `learning.person_view.*` key, `learning.notes_review`, `learning.import_claude_notes` and `learning.use_claude_instructions` (`privacy.vendor_cloud.*` is already covered by `privacy.*`). This list is a constant in `eileen-config` (prefixes such as `desktop.` cover every key under them), and the registry test fails if any key it covers lacks risk `Safety`, so a key added later under one of these prefixes is safe by default. The product and repository stay Eileen; the user's assistant name, voice and personality are the user's own.

**Rename and personality** ("I want to call you Nova now", "be funnier") change `assistant.*`, which the addressee gate, the recognizer hint list, the phrase cache and the per-turn `[assistant]` block read. Because personality travels in the turn, not the system prompt, the change applies on the next turn with no brain restart; the cache re-renders phrases that contain the name.

### 2.12 Permission tiers and security

| Tier | Name | Examples | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Observe | DesktopState, a screenshot, read a file in the job's folder, read a page, read a job | Silent |
| 1 | Reversible | Move or snap a window, change the volume, open an app, a file or a site, play or pause, edit files in the job's repository, create a branch, merge locally, add a Resolve version and an edit on top of it, insert dictated text | At once, journaled with its inverse |
| 2 | Consequential | Send a message, delete outside a repository, push, install software, spend credits, change settings outside Eileen, speak as the user to others, publish a scenario or results, any shell command not on the allowlist, read outside the job's folder, open a file that runs code, run a repository's build or tests after the job read untrusted content, network egress once a job is tainted and has read private data (section "One URL rule") | Confirm first |
| 3 | Blocked | Enter credentials, make payments, disable security features, bypass a CAPTCHA, read vendor credential files or Eileen's own data folder (beyond the job's own folders and snapshot), type text that looks like a secret or a card number | Never; explain and hand the step to the user |

Every tool declares its tier in its `ToolSpec`, per op where a tool has ops; a few depend on arguments (a control named Send is tier 2; a blocked combo is tier 3). An undeclared tool, including any tool of an approved third-party MCP server the user has not relaxed, is tier 2.

**Worker built-in tools** are classified by a deterministic policy in the core (`eileen_core::tiers::claude_tool_tier`), from facts the runtime adds to each `PreToolUse` request before the core sees it (the core does no IO, so the runtime canonicalises every path argument and classifies it, expanding `%VAR%`, `$env:VAR`, `$VAR` and `~` against the worker's environment and following junctions, symbolic links, 8.3 short names and `.lnk` targets to their final path, which it reads with `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` from a handle on the deepest part of the path that exists and then extends with the parts that do not; a `\\?\` or `\??\` path is taken as written, because Windows does not normalise it; a file with more than one hard link is classified under each of its names and takes the strictest class, because its final path is only the name it was opened by; a UNC path to this machine (`localhost`, a loopback address or the computer's own name) is classified as the local path it names when the share is an administrative drive share such as `C$`, and is `Unknown` (tier 2, HUD only, like an unparseable command) for any other share of this machine; any other UNC path is `Elsewhere`). The same classes apply to the path arguments of Eileen's own tools from every origin (fields marked with the `x-eileen-path` schema extension, such as `apps_open_path`, `apps_explorer` `OpenFolder`, `apps_image` and `apps_photoshop` paths): a credential, Eileen-data or browser-profile path is tier 3 for them too, and a worker's read of an `Elsewhere` path sets `private_read`. A built-in tool outside the job kind's list (section 3.3.9), and every subagent tool, is tier 3.

| Path class | Read, Grep, Glob | Edit, Write |
|---|---|---|
| `JobFolder`, `JobTemp`: the job's repository or worktree, its scratch folder (`run\<session>\cwd`), the job's temp folder | 0 | 1 |
| `GitInternals`: a path inside the job's own folders (`JobFolder` or `JobTemp`) with a component named `.git` below the folder's root (a directory or a worktree's pointer file, so a nested repository's `sub\.git\config` and a rewritten `.git` file are both caught), plus the job repository's git directory and common directory wherever they live (a worktree's `gitdir` target, found when the job starts by reading its `.git` file and `commondir`, never by running git), including `hooks\`, `config`, `info\attributes` and `modules\`. A `.git` component is recognised the way git's own `core.protectNTFS` recognises one (`.git` in any ASCII case, then only dots and spaces up to the end of the component or an alternate-data-stream suffix such as `::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` or `:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION`), and also by either short name NTFS gives `.git` (`GIT~<n>`, and `GI`, four hex digits and `~<n>` once `GIT~1` to `GIT~4` are taken). The rule is applied to the path as Windows will read it (after `GetFullPathNameW`, except for a `\\?\` or `\??\` path) and again to its final path, because a junction into `.git` shows only in the final path and a `.git` that is itself a junction only in the path as written; and a folder that holds `HEAD`, `objects\` and `refs\` (git's own test for a git directory: the target of such a junction or of a `gitdir:` file, or a bare repository) counts as a `.git` component. [2026-10-wp2-dotgit-spellings.md](research/2026-10-wp2-dotgit-spellings.md) has the measured spellings | 0 | 2, HUD only |
| `SkillSnapshot`: the session's own learned-skill snapshot (`run\<session>\plugin`) | 0 | 3 |
| `Notes` (D77): only the notes folders the session's `MemorySpec` names: its own repository's note folder (`<data_dir>\memory\claude-repos\<repo-hash>\`) and, for a session that holds the hub's index (a coding session), Claude's notes hub (`<data_dir>\memory\claude\`). Every other path under `memory\` is `EileenData` | 0, and sets `private_read` | 1 or held, by section 2.10 item 15's rules, only for a note-pattern file directly in the session's own memory folder when it writes natively; 3 for the hub, instruction-file names, subfolders, other file types and shell writes |
| `Toolchain`: cargo registry and git, rustup toolchains, npm global modules, uv cache | 0 | 2 |
| `EileenData`: every other path under Eileen's data folder (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen`, including other sessions' folders, other jobs' worktrees, the notes folders the session's `MemorySpec` does not name, `run\bus.json` and `run\session.json`); `Credential`: credential stores (`.ssh`, `.aws`, `.config\gh`, `.git-credentials`, `.netrc`, vendor CLI credential files, Windows credential folders); `BrowserProfile`: browser profile folders | 3 | 3 |
| `Elsewhere`: anywhere else | 2 | 2 |

The classes are tried in one fixed order and the first whose rule matches wins: `Credential` and `BrowserProfile` (tier 3 wherever they sit), then `GitInternals` (whose rule includes lying inside the job's own folders or git directories), then `SkillSnapshot`, then `Notes`, then `JobFolder` and `JobTemp`, then `EileenData`, then `Toolchain`, then `Elsewhere`. A `.git` component anywhere else (another repository on the disk, another job's worktree, a git checkout in the cargo registry) does not make a path `GitInternals`: the path keeps the class it has without that rule, so a read of `D:\other\.git\config`, which often holds remote URLs with tokens in them, is `Elsewhere` (tier 2, asked, and it sets `private_read`), and another job's worktree's `.git` file is `EileenData` (tier 3); a write to such a path takes the higher of its class's write tier and tier 2, HUD only (`PathFact.git_component`). The class is decided on the final path, so a junction `ext` in the job folder to another repository makes `ext\.git\config` that repository's configuration (`Elsewhere` with `git_component`), not a tier 0 read. So an in-place job's `.git\hooks\pre-commit`, inside the job folder, is `GitInternals`; a worktree under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen\worktrees\` is the job's folder for that job and Eileen's data for every other job; and the session's snapshot is never Eileen's data for its own session. Every write a worker makes anywhere under the user's `.claude` folder is tier 3 (D77), so a note can never land where the owner's own sessions load it; the Codex and Grok routes add `~/.codex` and `~/.grok` to this rule when they are built, and Grok's credential file joins `Credential`.

**Git runs code too.** A repository's hooks, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `core.sshCommand`, credential helpers, filter drivers, `diff.<driver>.textconv`, `diff.external` and `!` aliases all run programs on ordinary git commands (`git status` can run an fsmonitor hook, `git diff` a textconv driver), and git runs the configuration of whichever repository it finds, which need not be the job's. So:

- Writes into `GitInternals` (a `.git` component inside the job's folders, the job's own git directory) are tier 2 and HUD only, and so are writes to a `.git` path of any other class (at that class's tier when it is higher), through the file tools, shell redirections and every other path, so a worker cannot build a nested repository's configuration or hooks, or point a worktree's `.git` file at a directory it made, without the user.
- `git config` in any form that sets, unsets, adds or edits a value, `git -c <key>=...`, and the global options that choose another repository, configuration or program (`--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--config-env`, `--exec-path`, `--namespace`, `--bare`) are tier 2 and HUD only. `git -C <path>` is treated like `cd <path>`: the path is classified, and the rules below apply to the repository found there.
- Environment assignments choose what runs too: `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` makes git load a `.gitconfig` the worker wrote as its global configuration (it can set `diff.external`, a textconv driver or `core.hooksPath`, and it is not a `.git` path, so writing it is an ordinary job-folder write), `GIT_*` variables reconfigure git directly, and `EDITOR`, `GIT_EDITOR`, `PATH` or `NODE_OPTIONS` pick the program a command starts. So any environment assignment in a command line makes the whole command tier 2 and HUD only, whatever the command: a `VAR=value` prefix, `env VAR=value`, `export`, `set` and `setx`, `$env:VAR = ...`, `Set-Item env:...` and `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable`. The only exceptions are five variables that name no program, file or folder (`RUST_BACKTRACE`, `RUST_LOG`, `CARGO_TERM_COLOR`, `NO_COLOR` and `CI`) set to one word with no path separator, quote or `$`, which leave the command's tier as it was.
- Every worker's environment pins `core.fsmonitor=false` and `safe.bareRepository=explicit` through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2` and `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` (command-scope configuration outranks the repository's), so no fsmonitor hook runs and git never adopts a bare repository it merely finds in a folder; this costs only speed.
- For every git command, the runtime finds the repository the command will use by walking up from the command's working folder (tracked through `cd`, `git -C` and `&&` chains) to the first `.git` entry, never by running git, and adds a `GitFact` to the `PreToolUse` request (section 3.3.3): whether that repository is the job's own, and whether it has hooks (any non-sample file in its hooks directory, or `core.hooksPath` set in its configuration file). Hooks that were already in the job's repository are trusted as much as the repository itself, like its build scripts, and writes to a hooks directory always ask, so a hook present at call time was either there at the start or approved. But once the job is tainted, the git commands that run hooks (`commit`, `merge`, `pull`, `checkout`, `switch`, `rebase`, `cherry-pick`, `am`) are tier 2 when that repository has any hook, for the same reason build and test commands are; and once tainted, any git command whose repository is not the job's own (a nested or vendored repository) is tier 2, because its configuration can run drivers on `git diff` or `git add`.

**Shell commands** (`Bash`, `PowerShell`) are parsed with a small tokenizer (never evaluated) and default to tier 2. An allowlist makes the usual work tier 0 or 1: read-only commands (`git status`, `git diff`, `git log`, `ls`, `dir`, `cat`, `type`, `rg`, `grep`), local git (`add`, `commit`, `branch`, `switch`, `checkout`, `stash`, `merge`, `worktree list`, subject to "Git runs code too" above), `cd` into a folder of the job, and build and test commands inside the job's folder (`cargo build|check|test|clippy|fmt|xtask`, `npm test`, `npm run test`, `pnpm test`, `pytest`, `python -m pytest`, `uv run pytest`, `go test`, `dotnet build|test`). A command line holding an environment assignment is never on the allowlist ("Git runs code too" above). Paths in commands are classified like file-tool paths: the tokenizer marks every path-like argument (anything with a slash or backslash, a drive letter, `~`, an environment-variable reference, or a bare word that names an existing file relative to the command's working folder, tracked through `cd` and `&&` or `;` chains), the runtime classifies each into a `PathFact`, and the command's tier is the higher of its own tier and the path table's (the Read column for reads, the Edit and Write column for writes and redirections such as `>`, `>>`, `Out-File` and `Set-Content`). So `type %LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen\run\bus.json` or `cat ~/.ssh/id_rsa` is tier 3, and `rg secret C:\Users\<name>\Documents` is tier 2 and sets `private_read`. A glob (`*` and `?`; `[...]` in PowerShell, except as a `-LiteralPath` value, and in Git Bash, which also expands `{a,b}` and expands globs in redirections; PowerShell's `-Path` with `-Force` matches the hidden `.git`), an environment variable that does not resolve, a drive-relative path (`D:x`) on a drive other than the working folder's, or a Git Bash path the runtime cannot translate makes the path unknown and the command unparseable. In a `Bash` command, `/c/x` is `C:\x`, `//host/share` is a UNC path, and Git's own mounts (`/tmp`, `/usr`) are translated through Git's mount table, because `GetFullPathNameW` reads `/c/x` as `\c\x` on the current drive. A command line that the active code page's best-fit mapping changes is unparseable too: an ANSI program receives U+FF0E as `.`, U+FF3C as `\` and U+FF02 as `"`, so it opens `sub\.git\config` for a fullwidth spelling that names nothing for Eileen's own wide-character view. Known egress binaries and subcommands (`curl`, `wget`, `Invoke-WebRequest`, `Invoke-RestMethod`, `ssh`, `scp`, `gh`, `git push|fetch|pull|clone|remote add`, `npm install|publish`, `pip install`, `cargo install|publish`, `winget`) are tier 2; security settings (`Set-ExecutionPolicy`, `netsh`, `bcdedit`, `reg add` under policy keys, `icacls` and `takeown` outside the job's folder) are tier 3. A command the tokenizer cannot fully parse (command substitution, `-EncodedCommand`, `Invoke-Expression`, `iex`, unknown parts in a pipeline, globs and unresolved variables in path positions) is tier 2 and can be approved only by a HUD click, never by voice, because its spoken summary would not be trustworthy.

**Build and test commands run code.** `cargo test`, `npm test`, `pytest` and the rest execute code the worker may have edited (build scripts, `package.json` scripts, tests) with the user's network access and the user's git, GitHub CLI and SSH credentials, which neither the tokenizer nor the environment denylist can see (the denylist removes tokens from the environment, not the credential helper or the SSH agent). Two rules narrow this: the allowlisted build and test commands are tier 1 only while the job is untainted; once it has read anything from an observed source (below), each of them is tier 2, asked once per job and command ("Claude wants to run the tests, which runs code it changed after reading a web page. Allow?"). Beyond that, running the repository's own code is trusted as much as the repository is; this is a known v0.1 limit (section 7), and a real sandbox is v0.2 (decision D40).

**Taint.** Each job carries a taint record in the core (`Taint`, section 3.3.2): the observed sources it has read (`ObservedSource`: the desktop tree, a screenshot, the selection or clipboard, web content, a file outside its folder, a notification), from which `untrusted_seen` follows, and `private_read` (after it read anything outside its folder and the learned-skill snapshot, by any tool, shell commands and Eileen's own path tools included, any note in the `Notes` class among them; a session that loaded the hub's index through its prompt file holds private data from its start, D77). This is the same set the learning loop uses to mark tainted steps and hold drafts (section 2.10, item 9); there is no second, narrower list.

**What counts as an observed read.** One pure function, `eileen_core::tiers::observed_source(tool, op, args, paths, ctx) -> Option<ObservedSource>`, table-tested by WP1, decides it for every call from a worker, the brain or a replay, before the call runs (the taint is set when the call is allowed, so a result can never arrive untainted), and the same table tells the harness which results to keep as observed text for provenance (`WorkerEvent::ToolResult.observed`, redacted, at most 16 KB) and the dispatcher which of Eileen's own results to keep:

| Call | Source |
|---|---|
| `desktop_tree`, `desktop_state` (window titles and the focused element) | `Tree`, except `Web` when the window is a browser and the tree includes its web content (the `Dom` rung of section 2.6), and `Web` for any window of an app in the mail, chat or social category of `app-categories.toml` (message bodies are third-party free text) |
| `desktop_windows` (every top-level window's title), `desktop_wait_for` (it returns the element or window that matched) | by the same target rule: `Tree`, or `Web` when a listed or matched window belongs to an app in the mail, chat or social category or the match is in a browser's web content |
| The verification hint of any desktop action (`ToolResult.verification.observed`: the value, name or title read back after `desktop_click_element`, `desktop_set_value`, `desktop_type_text`, `desktop_press_keys`, `desktop_click`, `desktop_scroll`, `desktop_drag`, `desktop_window` and `desktop_launch_app`) | by the same target rule for the window acted on; an action whose verification reads back only Eileen's own values (a window rectangle, a volume level) is none |
| `apps_media` `status` (the media session's title and artist, set by whoever published the media) | `Web` |
| `apps_recent` (media-history titles and URLs, recent document names) | `Web` when `kinds` includes media or is empty, else `File`; its URLs and paths still count as built by Eileen's code for the URL rule below, because the media history and the recent-work index record only what the user opened |
| `desktop_screenshot`, `desktop_look` | `Screen` |
| `desktop_selection`, the clipboard fallback | `Selection` |
| `WebFetch`, `WebSearch`, `routes_lookup` (registry descriptions anyone can publish) | `Web` |
| `Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`, and shell read commands (`cat`, `type`, `rg`, `grep`, `Get-Content`) whose path is outside the job's folder, its snapshot and its temp folder; `apps_office` and `apps_explorer` reads of a document's text or selection | `File` |
| Notification hooks and toasts read by a worker | `Notification` |
| Claude in Chrome tools (only in a job the user opted in with `chrome`) | `Web`, every tool, reads and actions alike |
| Tools of an approved third-party MCP server | the `observes` class recorded with the server in `mcp.servers.<app>` at approval (default `Web`; the user may record `Tree` for a server that only reports the app's own state, such as ResolveMCP), for every tool of that server |
| An egress shell command the user approved (`curl`, `Invoke-WebRequest`, `gh`, `git fetch` and the rest of the egress list) | `Web` |
| An `Act` job's own brain turn (the job starts with what that turn's context held, below), and `ledger_search` results | `Tree` for a `[desktop]` block or ledger lines, `Web` when they include a window of a mail, chat or social app or a browser's web content, and each `[jobs]` line's source job's sources |
| `jobs_status` (each listed job's narration, summary and question lines, which its worker wrote) | the same rule as a `[jobs]` line: the union of the listed jobs' own `Taint.sources`, none when none of them read anything from outside (`ByJobs`) |
| Anything else (Eileen's computed results: timecode math, sharpness, job ids and states; the user's own Resolve projects and timelines; settings and learned items) | none |

Each tool's `OpSpec` declares the class of its own ops (`observes`, which covers everything the op returns, its verification hint included: `ByTarget` for the desktop rows above, `ByArgs` for `apps_recent`, `ByJobs` for `jobs_status`), the third-party and Chrome rows come from configuration, and the shell rows from the tokenizer, so the function needs no list of its own beyond this table. An op that returns text neither the user nor Eileen wrote must declare a class other than none; WP1's table test walks every `ToolSpec` and fails on an op whose output schema has a free-text field and whose rule is `None`, unless `contracts/observes-none.toml` lists that op with the reason (the user's own Resolve project names, Eileen's settings).

**An `Act` job starts with its turn's taint.** A brain turn is not a call, but its context already holds observed text: the `[desktop]` block's window titles and focused element, any ledger lines and the `[jobs]` block's worker lines. So when a brain turn runs `act` tools, the core starts the `Act` job's `Taint.sources` with the sources that context held (by the row above), and sends the text of those blocks, redacted and capped at 16 KB, with the job's `LearnCmd::Begin` (its `observed` field), which `eileen-learn` stores as the job's observed-text blob exactly as it stores a read tool's result. Its steps are therefore tainted from the start, a literal in a draft from that job that came from a window title traces as `Observed`, and the eight-token overlap sees the titles; the librarian still receives those blocks only as fenced observed data. The job's `private_read` is not set, because the brain's exits rule below already treats every brain call as holding private data.

**One URL rule.** Every URL check below is one pure function, `eileen_core::tiers::url_vouched(fact: &UrlFact, ctx: &UrlCtx) -> UrlVerdict` (section 3.3.3), table-tested by WP1 and called by the brain's exits, the dispatch rule and the worker's after-taint egress alike. A URL is vouched only when one of these holds: (1) the whole URL was built by Eileen's code (a grammar entry or resolver, or a built-in URL builder such as v0.1's video-page one) or copied verbatim from Eileen's media history or recent-work index; (2) its host was named in the authorizing turn's transcript (or the job's dispatched task, once that dispatch passed this rule) and its path, query and fragment are empty or `/`, or appear verbatim in that transcript, that task or an untainted step's arguments; or (3) the full URL, path and query included, is one the user visited, from the media history or the browser part of the ledger. A host the user has merely visited vouches for nothing beyond rule 3: data split across short path segments, or carried in a query, to a visited host is not vouched. And a host whose pages appear in the current turn's own context (the foreground window, or any window title in the turn's `[desktop]` block) is vouched only by the transcript, never by rule 3, because that page may be the source of the text asking for the visit; a verbatim copy from the media history or the recent-work index (rule 1) adds nothing to what is already there, so it still counts.

**What the rule reads.** The core does no IO, so the runtime attaches a `UrlFact` for every URL in a call before the core sees it, the way it attaches `PathFact`s: the URL of a `WebFetch`, the target of `apps_open_url` and `apps_open_uri`, the URL arguments of a shell command, and every URL and host the tokenizer finds in a `jobs_dispatch` call's `task`, `subtasks` and `subquestions`. For each it looks the URL up in the apps sidecar's media history and recent-work index (`in_index`, rule 1) and in the media history and the ledger's browser lines, which record the address-bar URL of each focused browser window (`visited`, rule 3: the full URL, path and query included). The core builds the `UrlCtx` from its own state: whether its own code built the URL (a grammar entry, a resolver, a built-in URL builder, or a replay step's pointer into an `apps_recent` URL), the authorizing turn's transcript, the job's dispatched task once that dispatch passed, the string arguments of the job's untainted steps, and the context hosts. For the brain's exits and a dispatch, the context hosts are the address-bar hosts of the foreground window and of every browser window in that turn's `[desktop]` block (`WindowInfo.url_host`, read over UI Automation by the desktop server, the same read the category rule of section 2.6 uses); for a worker's egress, they are those of the turn that dispatched the job plus the host of every page the job itself read (its `WebFetch` URLs, Claude in Chrome pages, browser windows its desktop reads touched). When a browser window in that context has an address bar that could not be read, the context is incomplete and rule 3 vouches for nothing in that check, so the rule fails closed; the verdict names the rule that vouched or the reason it did not (`VouchRule`, `UrlRefusal`).

Once a job is both tainted and `private_read`, every call that can send data out is tier 2: `WebFetch`, `apps_open_url` or `apps_open_uri` to a URL that `url_vouched` does not vouch for, a `WebSearch` whose query does not appear verbatim in the authorizing transcript, the dispatched task or an untainted step, `apps_open_path` on a file whose registered handler is a web browser (`.html`, `.htm`, `.xhtml`, `.mht`, `.svg`) or on a `.url` or `.website` shortcut (classified by the URL inside), desktop text input, paste or Enter in a browser window (asked once per job and host, the host read from the address bar), and any shell command not on the allowlist. A simulator invariant checks it. Where the task names a site that Eileen can vouch for (the dispatch rule below), `WebFetch` is restricted to that domain for the job (Claude's domain rules in `--allowedTools`).

**The brain's exits.** A brain turn always holds untrusted text (window titles) and private data (Claude's notes, the ledger), so its own `apps_open_url` and `apps_open_uri` calls are tier 1 only when `url_vouched` (above) vouches for the target: built by Eileen's code or copied verbatim from the media history or the recent-work index, a host named in this turn's transcript with a path, query and fragment that are empty or verbatim in the transcript, or a full URL the user visited, never by that last rule for a host whose page is in this turn's own context, and never by it while a browser window in that context has an address bar that could not be read. Every other URL from the brain is tier 2, so neither a window title that says "open evil.example/?q=..." nor one that says "open evil.example/<the facts>" on a page the user has visited can carry data out unasked. The same rule covers what the brain hands to a worker: the dispatcher extracts every URL and host from a `jobs_dispatch` call's `task`, `subtasks` and `subquestions` (the tokenizer's URL and host patterns), and the dispatch is tier 1 only when `url_vouched` vouches for each URL and each bare host was named in this turn's transcript or comes from Eileen's own resolvers; otherwise the whole dispatch is tier 2, with a summary Eileen builds from the parsed hosts ("Claude wants to research evil.example. Allow?"), and an unvouched host never becomes the job's `WebFetch` domain rule. So a brain turn that read a poisoned window title cannot start a worker that fetches an attacker's URL carrying the facts block, even though the worker itself starts untainted.

**A dispatched task can carry private text.** The worker's own facts block is bound by Eileen and scoped to the job, but the brain can also copy ledger lines, or facts outside the job's scope, into the task it writes. So the dispatcher starts the job with `private_read` set whenever the dispatching turn held any ledger lines (the block a past-referring utterance brings in, or `ledger_search` results), whatever the task says, because a paraphrase defeats any overlap test; ledger lines reach a turn only when the utterance refers to the past or the brain searches the ledger, so ordinary requests are not slowed. Notes are compared instead, because every turn holds some: the job also starts with `private_read` when its `task`, `subtasks` or `subquestions` share a run of four or more tokens with a line of the hub index in the brain's system prompt or a note body in the turn's `[notes]` block that the job's own note lines would not carry, or contain such a note's value (a name, number, date or path) verbatim (D77). A note the brain paraphrases passes that test, which section 7 names as a v0.1 limit. Such a job's later egress after any outside read then follows the after-taint rules above, so a computer-use job that types that text into a browser after reading a page is asked first. A deep-answer job whose task carries only its topic and in-scope preferences starts without it, so ordinary research is not slowed.

**Containment in v0.1** (decision D40, which amends D14): the job's repository or worktree, the path guard, the shell policy, taint, the `PreToolUse` guard failing closed, per-app desktop grants and Job Objects. The CLI's `--restricted` mode (section 2.5) is the second fence if WP3's test shows workers still work under it, and stays the second fence for any kind where it works, even though it turns the CLI's auto memory off, until the owner answers D77's question on that trade; other sandbox and Windows container options are measured by WP3 and recorded; a real sandbox and encryption at rest are v0.2.

**Credential boundary.** Eileen spawns the unmodified, installed `claude` binary and never reads, copies, logs or refreshes vendor credentials, never runs a login flow, and never writes the user's Claude settings for workers (all worker configuration is per-session flags). Claude's notes for Eileen's sessions live in Eileen's own data directory (section 2.10 item 15), so building on them needs no read of the user's `.claude` folder; D77 proposes one narrow exception, the user's own Markdown notes and instruction files read after a yes by `eileen_learn::vendor_notes` only, which waits for the owner, and until he answers no code path reads there. The binary is resolved to the real `.exe`: on this PC `claude` on `PATH` is the npm shim (`%APPDATA%\npm\claude.cmd` and `claude.ps1`), which points at `node_modules\@anthropic-ai\claude-code\bin\claude.exe`; the resolver parses the npm shim and the native installer's versioned layout and never picks the copy bundled inside the Claude desktop app. `eileen doctor` checks sign-in with `claude auth status` (exit code), never by reading files, and compares the installed CLI version with the one the brain is running. Because the always-on brain and spare keep the executable open, which can block an npm update on Windows, Eileen offers an update window: it drains workers, stops the brain and spare, lets the update run, and restarts them (WP3 verifies the behaviour once).

**Environments.** Two policies, built by `eileen-harness`:

- **Brain, librarian and scribe** (no shell): a constructed allowlist. It starts empty and adds only `SystemRoot`, `SystemDrive`, `windir`, `USERPROFILE`, `HOMEDRIVE`, `HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `TEMP`, `TMP`, `PATH` (system directories, git, the claude install directory), `PATHEXT`, `COMSPEC`, `NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS`, `PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE`, `OS`, plus the session's own variables.
- **Workers** (they run the user's builds and tests): the user's own environment, with system plus user `PATH` read from the registry so a freshly installed toolchain is seen, plus `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0=core.fsmonitor`, `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0=false`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_1=safe.bareRepository` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_1=explicit` (section "Git runs code too"), minus a denylist: every `CLAUDE*`, `ANTHROPIC*`, `MCP_*`, `OPENAI*`, `CODEX_*`, `XAI_*` and `DEEPSEEK*` variable, anything ending in `_API_KEY`, `_TOKEN` or `_SECRET`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `AWS_*`. A short pass-through list survives the denylist: `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` (the CLI uses it to find Git Bash on Windows), `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` and `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`. For coding jobs, toolchain folders Eileen can detect (`%USERPROFILE%\.cargo\bin`, Node, uv) are prepended when missing. Then the session's own variables are added.

A tidy check fails if any source names a vendor credential path or the persistent MCP registration command.

**Untrusted input.** Window titles, UIA text, page text, file contents, transcripts of other voices, tool results and model output are data. In the contracts they are wrapped as `Untrusted<T>` or `Personal<T>` (section 3.3.1), and a test fails if a free-text field reachable from `Input`, `ToolResult` or `DesktopState` is left unwrapped. They can never approve, raise a tier, create an override, start a job from an ambient turn, or become a skill without the user's approval. The core's approval type can only be built from `UserInput`.

**Who is calling.** Tokens sit in files any process of the same user can read (`run\<session>\mcp.json`, `run\bus.json`), so a token alone proves nothing:

- The dispatcher calls `GetNamedPipeClientProcessId` on every pipe connection and accepts the brain, worker, librarian and dev roles only from an `eileen-shim.exe` from Eileen's own install folder running inside the Job Object of the session that token belongs to (`IsProcessInJob` with the supervisor's job handle). A worker that copies the brain's token is still inside its own job and is refused.
- The `session` role is the opposite case, because the owner's Claude app starts that shim from the plugin's `.mcp.json`: it is accepted only from an `eileen-shim.exe` from the install folder that runs outside every Eileen-started Job Object, presenting the per-boot session token, which the shim reads itself from `run\session.json` (user-only ACL, separate from `bus.json` and every worker token). It gets only the `session` toolset, every call it makes is ambient, and the brain, worker, librarian and dev tokens are refused on it. A shim inside any Eileen job presenting the session token is refused, so a worker cannot reach the session tools.
- The loopback server maps each client socket to its process (`GetExtendedTcpTable`). Hook endpoints accept only a process inside the matching session's job; every other endpoint (state, control, `say`, `handoff`) refuses any process inside any Eileen-started Job Object.
- `eileen say` enters as `TurnSource::Cli`, which can never answer an approval, confirm a safety setting or create a grant, and gets read-only brain tools (Turn authority, section 2.3).
- The path guard denies workers every part of Eileen's data folder (tier 3) except the job's own worktree, `cwd` and temp folder, the session's own snapshot (read only) and the notes folders its own `MemorySpec` names, under the `Notes` rules (so a web or screen worker never reads the hub, and a coding job never reads another repository's notes), by the most-specific-class rule above, through the file tools, through path arguments in shell commands and through Eileen's own path tools alike; so a worker can never read `bus.json`, `session.json`, another session's tokens or another job's worktree.

WP2's acceptance includes a process inside a worker Job Object, holding every token file, being refused by every endpoint and every other role's pipe, and the session role's rule both ways.

**Loopback endpoint.** Binds 127.0.0.1 only on a random port, with a per-boot bearer token in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen\run\bus.json` (user-only ACL). That token reaches status and control (mute, say) only; no endpoint reachable with it can approve anything.

**Secrets.** Only one is allowed now (the DeepSeek key, used by no v0.1 route), plus connector tokens later. They sit behind the `SecretStore` platform trait (section 3.4): `eileen-config` asks the trait, and `eileen-shell-win` implements it on Windows Credential Manager through keyring-core 1.0.1 and windows-native-keyring-store 1.1.0 (a Linux port implements it on Secret Service). Secrets are typed as `secrecy::SecretString` and are never in config files, logs or model-visible text.

**Privacy.** Everything stays on the PC except what a Claude session sends to the owner's own plan.

- Disclosure by data class (decision D79, after v0.1): `privacy.classes.<id>` keys, Safety keys like every `privacy.*` key, say per data class whether Eileen may speak it, show it, give it to which routes and send it off the PC. Before speaking anything of a class whose rule limits speech, the core checks the audience state (alone, others present, or in a call) and whether a private screen is available, and shows the answer there instead. A rule may also carry an optional `speaker_match` condition, which takes effect only while the speaker gate of section 2.8 is `on`, that is, only after a speaker check has passed D76's measured bar; until then a rule decides by the audience state and the private screen alone, and no command is refused for voice.

- Speech that was not addressed to Eileen stays in memory only: the HUD shows it for a few seconds, and every persisted sink (the `events` table, logs, the flight recorder's input lane when an incident or recording is written, scenarios) stores "[not addressed, n words]" instead, unless the user opts in. Partial transcripts never leave the core and the HUD state: `BusEvent::Heard` carries only a final transcript, and only once its turn is routed somewhere other than "ignored" (for an `[addressee: unsure]` turn, when the brain's first output is not `---ignore---`), so nothing the gate later rejects reaches the event log, the loopback endpoint or the plugin. Nothing heard while another app uses the microphone (a call) is stored at all.
- The `events` and `approvals` tables keep 30 days; trajectories, the ledger, recordings and incidents are local, size-capped and deletable by voice (section 2.15). Screenshots are not stored by default.
- The public repository never receives the owner's voice, screen or personal paths (tidy enforces it for fixtures and scenarios).
- The HUD's privacy note says plainly that Eileen can delete what it stores but not what a vendor retains.
- Claude's notes (section 2.10 item 15) are files in the data directory, machine-local and never synced; their text reaches Anthropic only inside a Claude session's prompt, like everything else a session sends to the owner's plan.
