HUD state assembly; scenario-save requests and the automatic local scenario for every failed job and replay handoff. `eileen-record`: the formats of section 3.3.16 (input records and hook answers as `RecordLine`s), the BLAKE3 and zstd blob store with reference counts, reader, writer, `trim` with setup inputs and with each kept hook input's answer. `eileen-replay`: strict mode with the first-divergence report, scenario mode with reactive responses and ID remapping, the expectation and expected-trace matcher, the `responder` component for `--replay-models`, and `eileen replay`. `eileen-replay` also implements scenario mode's three input rules (scheduled, reactive, synthesized; section 3.3.16) with the stable key of each reactive family (transcript digest and occurrence, never a global ordinal), the brain's and workers' tool calls and hooks replayed inside their turn's sequence (hooks held for the core's answer and a divergent decision failing the replay), and its own brain instance numbers stamped on every served brain event, and `eileen scenario replay`, which `cargo xtask scenarios replay` dispatches to. `eileen-sim`: the world of section 4.7 (with available memory, commit headroom and paging, the machine's facts, CPU load and power, a heavy app that grows and closes, and a stalling fake brain backend), profiles `default`, `barge-in-heavy`, `many-jobs`, `flaky-sidecars`, `media-playing`, `low-memory`, `heavy-app`, `roomy`, all 16 invariants, exhaustive turn-taking (depth 6 on pull requests, 8 nightly), `SimRegression` files. Once WP0's `wp0/machine-contracts` has merged, the machine profile's core part (section 2.16): `eileen_core::fit` with the plan (the budget, the memory rows in order, the class, the other rows from facts and measurements, the coarse `memory.profile` override, overrides, and `MachineFit` evaluated through the same function in the settings flow), the live re-check (items let go in reverse order 30 s apart, brought back after the 5-minute hold and never within 10 minutes, nothing brought back on battery saver), the two-summaries-then-quiet rule for model reloads, the worker cap on fan-out with its queue sentence, benchmark scheduling through `Effect::Machine`, `MachinePlanChanged` with its announcement row, the HUD chip and panel state, and `eileen_core::fit::words`, the templates for the summary, every reason and every chip line; `CoreConfig.machine`; and the speaker gate of section 2.8 (`eileen_core::speaker`: not adopted, shadow with its per-user counts, on, off; the live false-reject count; "stop checking voices"), with `CoreConfig.speaker_bar` and the snapshot fields of section 3.3.17. **From D77** (section 2.10 items 1, 2, 13 and 15; it waits for WP7's amendment that applies section 3.3.13's provisional shapes): the `Notes` rows of `claude_tool_tier` (tier 0 with `private_read` for a read inside the folders the session's `MemorySpec` names and `EileenData` for every other notes path; tier 1 or held, by the `NoteFact`, for a note-pattern write in the session's own folder; rejected for approval wording or a tombstone hit; tier 3 for instruction-file names, subfolders, other memory folders, shell writes into one and any write under the user's `.claude` folder); `url_vouched` vouching nothing found only in a note; the `[notes]` block as the notes after each instance's `NotesVersion` plus the bodies `notes::relevant` picks, within its caps; `private_read` on a dispatched job whose task repeats a hub index line its own note lines would not carry; make-before-break recycling for a forget of a note in an instance's starting index, with the quiet-moment fallback and the forget report pending until then; the `[match]` block for the first hit of an unconfirmed induced template at any level; the `Receipt` for every addressed request; and step corrections recorded on the `CallId` they interrupted. WP1 acceptance 17 tests them.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. All 16 invariants hold on the PR seed set and on 30 minutes of random seeds per profile (a `long.yml` run); each invariant has a hand-written scenario that breaks a fake and proves the invariant catches it (for invariant 15: a fake brain that stalls, then sends its whole answer after the fallback instance has started answering; only the fallback's answer is spoken).
  2. `turn::exhaustive` passes at depth 6 within the PR budget (depth 8 nightly).
  3. `tests/it/job_model.rs`: a proptest-state-machine reference model of the job lifecycle agrees with the core on random command sequences.
  4. Barge-in in simulation: onset to pause at most 96 ms of virtual time plus one callback; the heard prefix equals the text of the samples played; a backchannel resumes from the paused sample.
  5. Approval safety: a tool result, a worker's text, `eileen say` and a video's audio that each contain "yes, approve" do not approve; a bare "yes" and "Eileen, yes" do not approve a tier-2 call; "yes, push" in the window does, and with the speaker gate on it does only with a match; speech that began before the question finished playing does not; a HUD click flagged non-physical does not; an approve hotkey without the physical flag does not; an `approve` tool call whose `tool_use_id` matches no pending ask, or comes from another session, is refused and logged as a security event; an approval asked while a video plays is decided only after the pause is confirmed, and is voice-capable once the loopback is quiet.
  6. Turn authority and the permission table: a job-completion turn whose text says "dispatch a job" cannot dispatch one; a tainted job that read private data cannot `WebFetch` without approval; a table test of `claude_tool_tier` gives tier 3 for `Read` of `~/.ssh/id_rsa`, for `type %LOCALAPPDATA%\eileen\run\bus.json`, for `cat` through a junction into a credential folder and for `Task`; tier 2 (asked) for `Read` outside the job's folder and for `rg` over a Documents path (which also sets `private_read`); tier 2 HUD-only for a glob or an unresolved variable in a path position, and for `apps_open_path` on a `.bat`, a `.lnk` to PowerShell or with `with_app = "powershell"`; tier 0 or 1 for `cd sub && cargo test` inside the folder, and tier 2 for the same after the job read a web page; a brain `apps_open_url` to a host the user never named or visited is tier 2; a brain `jobs_dispatch` whose task names a host from a window title (not the transcript, never visited) is tier 2 with a summary naming the host, and one whose task names a host the user said is tier 1 with `WebFetch` narrowed to it; after taint and a private read, `WebFetch` to a visited host with a query string that is not in the transcript, task or an untainted step is tier 2, typing or Enter in a browser window is tier 2 (asked once per host), and `apps_open_path` on a job-folder `.html` or a `.url` is tier 2; `Write` to `.git\hooks\pre-commit` and `.git\config`, `git config core.hooksPath x`, `git -c core.fsmonitor=x status` and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=x git fetch` are tier 2 HUD-only; `git commit` after taint is tier 2 in a repository with a hook installed and tier 1 in one without; `Write` to `sub\.git\config` and to `sub\.git\hooks\post-checkout` inside the job folder (a nested repository), and to a worktree's own `.git` pointer file, are tier 2 HUD-only; in an in-place job, `.git\hooks\pre-commit` inside the job folder classifies as `GitInternals`, not `JobFolder`; `git --git-dir=x diff`, `git --work-tree=y status` and `git --config-env=core.pager=X log` are tier 2 HUD-only; `HOME=.\x git diff`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=x git diff`, `EDITOR=.\evil.bat git commit`, `env PATH=.\bin git status` and `$env:HOME='.\x'; git diff` are tier 2 HUD-only, while `RUST_BACKTRACE=1 cargo test` keeps the tier of `cargo test`; a `Read` of an unrelated repository's `D:\other\.git\config` is tier 2 (asked) and sets `private_read`, a `Write` to it is tier 2 HUD-only, and a read of a sibling job's worktree `.git` file is tier 3; after taint, `cd sub && git diff` in a nested repository (a `GitFact` with `job_repo = false`) is tier 2, while `git diff` in the job's own repository stays tier 0; `url_vouched` table over `UrlFact` and `UrlCtx`: a visited host with the data split across several short path segments and a visited host with a query are not vouched, nor is a full URL the user visited (`visited`) whose host is the foreground page's `url_host` or that of a browser window in the turn's `[desktop]` block, nor any `visited` URL while a browser window in that context has no readable address bar; a URL from the media history (`in_index`), a `visited` full URL whose host is in no context, and a host the user said with path `/` are vouched; each verdict names its `VouchRule` or `UrlRefusal`; a brain `jobs_dispatch` from a turn that held ledger lines starts its job with `private_read` set even when its task paraphrases them, one whose task repeats an out-of-scope fact's value does too, and one from a turn with no ledger lines whose task names only its topic does not; `Read` of the session's own snapshot is tier 0 and `Write` to it tier 3; a read of a sibling job's worktree or of `run\session.json` is tier 3; a replayed step calling `settings_apply` is refused; `ask_user` asking for a password, PIN, one-time code or card number is refused; `observed_source` gives the class of every row of section 2.12's observed-read table (a browser window's web-content tree and a mail-category window's tree are `Web`, a testpad tree is `Tree`, a Claude in Chrome read is `Web`, a third-party MCP tool is `Web` by default and `Tree` when recorded so, an approved `curl` and `routes_lookup` are `Web`, `desktop_windows` listing a mail-category window is `Web` and listing only the testpad `Tree`, a `desktop_set_value` on the testpad carries `Tree` through its verification hint, `apps_media` `status` is `Web`, `apps_recent` with `kinds = ["media"]` is `Web` and with documents only `File`, and an `Act` job created by a brain turn whose `[desktop]` block listed a mail window starts with `Web` in its taint and that block as its observed text), `jobs_status` listing a job that read a web page is `Web` and listing only jobs that read nothing is none; and the walk over every `ToolSpec` finds no free-text op with rule `None` outside `contracts/observes-none.toml`, and no tool with a selector whose variant repeats the name of one of the struct's other fields; fuzz target `shell_command` clean for 10 minutes.
  7. Two speeds: a deep answer arriving while the user talks goes to the HUD only; arriving in silence is spoken once.
  8. Memory admission: with simulated memory near the reserve, a third agent queues with a spoken reason naming the biggest process, starts by the paging rule once paging stays low for 30 s, or by "start it anyway"; commit headroom under 1 GB blocks even the override; the pool and spare are dropped first; a session that hits `worker_job_limit_mb` fails its job with the limit named.
  9. A recorded run replays in strict mode with no divergence; a deliberate change to the router makes it report the first divergent event with its sequence number; the same run saved as a scenario still passes in scenario mode after an unrelated change that mints one extra ID.
  10. `scenarios/feel/` (half-finished sentence with a pause, room talk ignored, conversation window with an unnamed request acted on, interrupt and resume, backchannel, "no, the other one" after a cancelled intent, video playing during an answer, an action and an approval), `scenarios/jobs/` (three parallel jobs, reroute, cancel, approval by voice with the action word, approval left to the deadline, memory-queued agent) and `scenarios/first-run/` (surprise accepted by click and by voice, consent unticked, clone too slow by setup's measurement runs the tour with nothing recorded, rendering past 40 s after the recording runs the tour and deletes the clip and the clone, a later tray "Show me something cool" records afresh, reveal declined deletes the clip and the clone and keeps only the listening calibration, "forget my voice" removes that too, and after every ending the phrasing store holds no demo phrasing while the demo's exact repeat still ran from L1) replay green.
  11. `eileen-core` depends only on `eileen-contracts`, `eileen-stdx` and `eileen-trace` (check 24).
  12. The learning decisions in the core, in simulation: "closer" after a deep answer stores a feedback fact scoped to its topic and refines from the cache; "no, my timelines start at zero" retires the old fact with the same subject key, says what it replaced, and queues a repair of the skills that list it; "undo that" right after a stored fact retires it as `Undone` with no repair queued, and "that wasn't a fact" does the same; with the store holding the alias "the video editor" = Resolve from a clarifying answer, "no, the video editor means Premiere" deletes that alias and stores Premiere, "open the video editor" then resolves to Premiere, and "undo that" restores Resolve as the only alias; "I don't like spicy food" retires an active "I like spicy food" of the same scope and leaves "I like ramen"; said scoped to a food answer it also retires an unscoped "I like spicy food", and said unscoped it retires a scoped one; "cheaper" after "closer" on the same answer leaves both active, while "further" retires "closer"; and a fact retiring as `Stale` drops no skill's level, while a successful use of a skill confirms the facts it lists; a deep answer whose link the user opened on the HUD records that host as accepted for its topic, and the next deep-answer job of that topic carries the sources line; a `learned_correct` whose instruction is not in the transcript is stored only after a HUD yes; "get Notepad up for me" said twice in ten seconds runs the second time from L1 (`ExactRepeat`), and an "undo that" after the first deletes the candidate; a clean regret window raises the candidate's `confirmed` count and a new phrasing never recycles the brain; the dispatcher marks `rung_skipped` for a vision step on an app with a declared API rung, and the next `JobBinding` for that app carries the route line; a forget completes only after both stores report, and a live session holding the skill is recycled; a level-3 template match produces a brain turn with the `[match]` block, and a `Replay` dispatch with different slots is refused; the coffee case of E1, said one second after Eileen finished, stays greyed inside the conversation window and produces no brain turn, while "and how long will it take?" after Eileen announced a render passes because "it" resolves to that job, and a question sharing a content word with her last answer passes too (the scripted resolver fake returns `FollowUp::Referent` and `FollowUp::Shared` for those cases and `None` for the coffee case); with a calibration whose `level_ref_db` is -30, an unnamed "make that bigger" inside the window at -42 dBFS (a quieter, farther voice) is greyed and produces no brain turn while the same words at -31 dBFS pass, and with no calibration the reference forms from the median of ten name-led turns and then greys the quieter voice the same way; "pause" while a video plays works without the name and "what's the time" does not; during a call, "pause" without the name does nothing, "Eileen, pause" pauses with no sound from Eileen, and "Eileen, what's the time?" is answered on the HUD only.
  13. Scenario mode: a saved scenario whose core change adds one timer, one extra clause and one extra embedding request still passes, because timers and playback are synthesized and the embedding is answered reactively; so does one after a change to the brain prompt's wording and to the `[jobs]` block's format, because brain turns are keyed by their source, the digest of the user's final transcript and its occurrence (section 3.3.16), never by the composed prompt, and one whose brain turn calls `jobs_dispatch` and `desktop_launch_app`, because the brain's tool calls are replayed within that turn's sequence; a recorded `Sidecar` `Exited` and a `Playback` `DeviceChanged` are scheduled; an effect with no recorded reaction is reported, not hung on. Also: a recorded stall-then-fallback (invariant 15's case) replays with the stalled and fallback sequences stamped with the replayer's own live instance numbers, and only the fallback's answer is spoken; a scenario recorded before an earlier request in it moved from the brain to L1 still matches its later brain turns and worker commands, because they are keyed by transcript digest and occurrence, not by ordinal; a pooled worker's `Init` is served after the live `Start(SessionSpec)` of the same class; a brain `Ready` after a live `Promote`; and a worker's recorded `PreToolUse` that the live core answers the same way as its `HookAnswerRecord` replays green, while one the live tier table now answers `Ask` fails with a hook divergence naming the tool and both decisions; `eileen record trim` keeps each kept hook input's answer; and the two sends of the stalled turn carry one key with send ordinals 1 and 2.
  14. The reserved job kind: a brain `jobs_dispatch` of kind `ml_project` ("train a model that finds a person's clothing colour on my Colab") is refused before routing and admission with a `UserError` of kind `JobKindUnavailable`, whose sentence names the next version and offers a research job on what already exists, returned to the brain as the call's error result; no session starts and no memory admission is asked for; a `research` dispatch with the same task is then accepted as usual.
  15. The speaker gate (section 2.8), in simulation with a scripted speaker score: with no `SpeakerBar`, and with one whose `passed` is false, no utterance in the conversation window, no alone-check request and no voice approval is ever refused for voice, whatever `SpeakerMatch` says, and the plan's staged row says why with the numbers; with a passing bar and an enrolled user the gate stays in shadow, refusing nothing, until 300 of the user's own turns (acted on with no correction in the regret window, or followed by the user's own click or hotkey approval) give a false-reject upper bound under 1 percent, and only then greys another voice and requires a match on a tier-2 voice approval; a turn greyed for voice and recovered by "that was for you" counts as a false reject, and the third within the last 300 gated turns turns the gate off with one spoken sentence, after which a voice approval needs the action word only; "stop checking voices" turns it off at once; `scenarios/feel/` gains an action-word approval with the check not adopted, which replays green.
  16. The machine plan (section 2.16), table-tested over WP0's three fixture machines with a session cost of 500 MB, and in simulation: the owner's shape with 1.8 GB usually free gives `Tight`, Nemotron and Kokoro fp32 (the budget covers both), the deep pool target 1 started by admission only, no spare, 4 speech threads and 6 workers at once; the same shape with 0.9 GB usually free takes Kroko only when the catalogue marks it adopted and within its command-accuracy bar, and otherwise keeps Nemotron with a negative budget and no warm session; the 32 GB fixture gives `Roomy`, the full models, the spare, a deep pool session, a computer-use target of 2 and two build-heavy jobs; the laptop fixture gives `Tight`, 2 speech threads where its fixture measurement found 2 fastest, and 4 workers; with fewer than 24 hours of history the first minute's highest reading decides; in the `heavy-app` profile on the 32 GB fixture, an app growing to 12 GB lets the computer-use session, then the spare, then the deep session go, 30 s apart, with chip lines naming the app and nothing spoken, and after it closes they return highest value first, 5 minutes later and never within 10 minutes of their drop; a recognizer change waits for two hourly summaries and then for a quiet moment, never during a turn, dictation or a call; "keep a spare brain anyway" on the owner's shape asks first with the cost, survives every re-plan and is held back only by rule 1, with the panel saying so; "let Eileen decide" returns the keys to `auto` and "undo that" restores them; no automatic benchmark is asked for during a call, dictation, a job or a held build slot, and one is asked for after the fixture's CPU changes; fan-out of eight on the owner's shape queues two children with the worker-cap sentence; the summary and every reason sentence for the three fixtures are insta snapshots built from templates; `scenarios/machine/` (the three fixture plans, the heavy app, the override) replays green.
  17. Notes and receipts (D77, section 2.10 item 15), in simulation and as table tests: `claude_tool_tier` gives tier 0 with `private_read` for a `Read` in the `Notes` class; tier 1 for a note-pattern `Write` in the session's own folder from an untainted, learning job whose `NoteFact` traces to the user's words; held (deny with the review reason) for the same write from a tainted job, from a desktop-tree-only job, and for an untraced feedback note; rejected for a note with approval wording or one a tombstone matches; tier 3 for `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md` in a memory folder, a subfolder, another session's memory folder, a shell write into one, and any write under the user's `.claude` folder; and `EileenData`, tier 3, for a `Read` of the hub by a computer-use, research or deep-answer worker and for a coding job's `Read` of another repository's note folder. `url_vouched` never vouches a URL found only in a note, and a tainted worker holding a note cannot `WebFetch` an unvouched URL without asking. The `[notes]` block carries exactly the notes after the instance's `NotesVersion` plus the relevant bodies, within its caps, and a dispatched task that repeats a hub index line the job's own note lines would not carry starts with `private_read`. A forget of a note in the brain's starting index with no spare starts the replacement through admission while the old instance serves with the steer, promotes it when ready, and, when admission refuses, recycles at the next quiet moment with the forget report pending until then. The first hit of an unconfirmed induced template reaches the brain as a `[match]` block for a level-2 and for a level-4 skill, and once confirmed the template routes by the skill's level. Every addressed request ends with a `Receipt` naming exactly its layer and records; each steer, "no, the other one" and confirm-step "no" lands on the `CallId` it interrupted.

### 6.4 WP2: Runtime, binaries, shell and the first slice

- **Branch** `wp2/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A and WP1's minimal core (its first pull request), and starts as soon as that merges; integrates WP3's `wp3/worker-brain` and WP4's `wp4/s1-audio` and `wp4/s1-speech` for slice S1. **Desktop** no for its automated tests (its few tray and hotkey tests take the desktop lock); slice S1 runs on the owner's PC. **Wave** W1 from WP1's minimal core; `wp2/s1-live` merges as soon as `wp3/worker-brain` and WP4's two slice pull requests have, in W1 when they are done in time; the rest of WP2 continues in W2.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-runtime`, `crates/eileen-mcp`, `crates/eileen-cli`, `crates/eileen-shim`, `crates/eileen-crash`, `crates/eileen-guard`, `crates/eileen-shell-win`, `crates/eileen-scenarios`, `TESTING.md`, `docs/DEBUGGING.md`, `scenarios/runtime/`, fuzz targets `mcp_client_message`, `mcp_tool_arguments`, `dispatch_line`, `worker_hook_payload`, `http_request` and `scenario_file` with their corpora and regressions, `docs/wp/WP2-runtime.md`.
- **Builds.** The driver loop (one task owns `Core`; inputs through a bounded channel; effects routed to components by family; tasks through `eileen_rt`); the SQLite store (jobs, events with `seq` and `TraceCtx`, approvals with their `ApprovalSource`, settings versions, journal, plan usage, memory samples; migrations; WAL; `secure_delete`; retention; `Record::PurgeJobs` with a `TRUNCATE` checkpoint and `StoreEvent::Purged`, and, with `logging.include_personal` on, rewriting log files without the purged jobs' and turns' lines); the bus fan-out with explicit `Lagged` and no unaddressed speech; the memory sampler (`Input::Memory` every 5 s from the `SystemMemory` trait, which `eileen-shell-win` implements: available memory, commit headroom, the paging rate through PDH converted from pages with the page size, the largest process; 30 s paging windows appended to the machine-wide `machine\paging-30s.jsonl` through `samples::append_paging` (marked `slot_held` when a build slot lock was held, one line per window however many runtimes run) and the `auto` limit recomputed hourly with `samples::auto_paging_limit`, and every sample also appended to the machine-wide `machine\memory-samples.jsonl` through `samples::append`, section 6.1; only a runtime reading real memory through `SystemMemory` writes either file, never one with a fake source); the loopback HTTP/1.1 server (strict limits, bearer token, client PID mapping and Job Object checks, `/v1/hooks/claude/<event>` including the `PermissionRequest` tripwire, `/v1/state`, `/v1/control/mute`, `/v1/say` as a `Cli` turn, `/v1/handoff` pending confirmation, `/v1/session/notify` for the plugin's non-gating hooks (session role's caller rule, a fixed set of notification kinds, rate-limited), `/v1/test/*` only under `--test-harness`, `bus.json`, `session.json`); path canonicalisation and classification into `PathFact`s for `PreToolUse`, for the shell tokenizer's path positions and for every Eileen tool field marked `x-eileen-path` (environment variables, `~`, junctions, symbolic links, 8.3 short names and `.lnk` targets resolved; the final path read from a handle on the deepest existing part, and every name of a file with several hard links classified; `\\?\` and `\??\` paths taken as written; UNC paths `Elsewhere`, except this machine's own shares (an administrative drive share is the local path it names, any other share `Unknown`); globs (with `[...]`, and Git Bash's `{a,b}`), unresolved variables, untranslatable Git Bash paths and command lines the best-fit mapping changes `Unknown`; the fixed class order of section 2.12 (credential and browser-profile paths, then a `.git` path component inside the job's folders, recognised by the `core.protectNTFS` rule and both short names on the path as written and on its final path, or a folder that holds a git directory, and the job's git and common directories, read from the worktree's `.git` file and `commondir` at job start, with `git_component` set on a `.git` path of any other class, then the session's snapshot, then the job's folders, then Eileen's data folder); a `GitFact` for every git command in a shell command line, found by walking up from its tracked working folder to the first `.git` entry without running git, with `job_repo` and `has_hooks`; a `UrlFact` for every URL in a call (a `WebFetch` URL, the targets of `apps_open_url` and `apps_open_uri`, a shell command's URL arguments, and every URL and host in a `jobs_dispatch` task, subtasks and subquestions, found with the core's tokenizer patterns), with `in_index` from the apps sidecar's media history and recent-work index and `visited` from the media history and the ledger's browser lines; `runs_code` and `opens_web` from the extension, the registered verb or handler and a shortcut's target); the dispatcher pipe server with caller verification and `eileen-shim` (section 3.3.9 protocol, toolsets per role, the session role's opposite rule and its own pipe and token, the `--notify <kind>` mode that posts to `/v1/session/notify` and always exits 0, `session_ask` waiting for the user's reply, `_meta.traceparent` passthrough, logging the `_meta` the CLI sends on its first call); sidecar MCP clients through rmcp over supervised stdio with deadlines, heartbeats, restart with backoff, and hang-dump-then-kill through the guard; the connector registry; the flight recorder wiring (input tap, checkpoints with RNG state, the per-job pin, placeholders for unaddressed speech, incident assembly, the janitor, the lag probe, the audio heartbeat watchdog); `eileen-crash` and `eileen-guard` (breakaway spawn, core heartbeat, hang dumps, rate-limited restart, fail-fast reporting); `eileen-shell-win` (the `SystemMemory` and `MachineIdentity` implementations, tray with data-driven menu and the physical-click check for unmute, global hotkeys including approve, deny and hands-off, Raw Input for the dictation key, physical activity and the "came from a device" flag for the approve, deny and unmute chords, the `SecretStore` implementation over keyring-core, toasts, the Start menu shortcut with the AppUserModelID, the HKCU Run entry); `eileen-cli` as the composition root wiring every component through `component(cfg)` and the real ports (stubs until their packages land), `--console`, `--replay-models`, `eileen about` with the credits generated from `NOTICE` and `models.toml`, the tray's "About Eileen" item, and the framework of `setup` and `doctor`; the install id and the machine hash (owner answer 6: the install id created by `setup` or the first `eileen run`, whichever comes first, and the hash recomputed at every start through `MachineIdentity` and `eileen_platform::machine::machine_hash`, then written to `fleet\machine.toml`, where scenario save, the speech process's consent records, the fleet runner and the benchmark runner read it); the `session` and `dev` toolset handlers (in `eileen-runtime::dev`, with `dev_replay_core` over `eileen-replay`; `session_skills` behind `claude_plugin.share_skills`); the incident hand-off for "explain that"; `eileen-scenarios` (save from the flight recorder, with the machine hash from `fleet\machine.toml` in `Scenario.machine`, the automatic local scenarios of section 2.10 item 13 with their caps, `check` through `eileen_trace::privacy` and `eileen_contracts::scenario::status_problems`, `bless` (section 3.3.16), `stale` (writing `since`, `issue` and `expires`), share with generalisation, redaction and the local scenario-mode replay, submit through the contributor's `gh` with the auto-filled template or the issue-form fallback, the real-desktop runner with fixtures, steps through the test endpoint, transcript injection or `WavSource`, expectation evaluation through the replay matcher and the sidecars' read-only ops, `ci_factor` timing); `TESTING.md`; `docs/DEBUGGING.md`; shuttle tests for the bus, supervisor, workers finishing together and a dropped shim pipe. EcoQoS opt-out at the start of every binary it owns. Once WP0's `wp0/machine-contracts` has merged, the machine profile's runtime part (section 2.16): `eileen_runtime::machine`, a component that reads `MachineProbe` at start (before the brain starts) and on its change events, joins the audio component's endpoints and the speech process's execution providers into the facts, sends `MachineEvent::Facts`, `Usual` (from `samples::usual`, after each hourly summary the sampler appends through `samples::append_hour`) and `Load` every 30 s (with whether a build slot lock is held), runs `DiskRead` and forwards the speech kinds of `Effect::Machine` to the speech process, turns `VoiceEvent::Bench` and `Providers` into `MachineEvent`s, builds `CoreConfig.machine` at start, and writes `machine\facts.toml`, `machine\bench.jsonl` and `machine\plan.toml` (only when reading the real machine); the `MachineProbe` implementation in `eileen-shell-win`, whose `windows` features join the root feature union by the one-line amendment of section 3.1; and `eileen doctor machine [--bench]`.
- **The slice's resolver.** `eileen-intent` is WP7's and arrives in W3, so `wp2/s1-live` adds a minimal real resolver, `eileen_runtime::bootstrap::SliceResolver`, a pure implementation of `IntentResolver` that the composition root wires until WP7 phase A's first pull request switches it to `eileen_intent::Resolver` and deletes it (an amendment to WP2's files): it matches the precedence-1 safety and control phrases the minimal core handles (mute, unmute, stop, cancel, "go on"), and "what time is it" as `CoreCommand::WhatTime`; everything else resolves to `Resolution::Brain`; `follow_up` returns `FollowUp::None` and `resolve_semantic` defers to `resolve`. A table test covers each phrase and a paraphrase that must reach the brain.
- **Slice S1, live** (`wp2/s1-live`, once `wp3/worker-brain`, `wp4/s1-audio` and `wp4/s1-speech` have merged, not all of WP3 and WP4; the gate for W3): `eileen run --console` on the owner's PC with the real microphone and speakers, holding `desktop.lock` for the run (`cargo xtask desktop-lock --hold`), so it never overlaps WP4's device tests or WP6's spike. One fast-path command ("Eileen, what time is it?"), one barge-in ("stop" while Eileen speaks), one brain answer, and one Claude job ("Eileen, create a file called hello dot txt containing hi in the scratch repo") whose completion is announced; the mute hotkey and the tray mute each stop capture and the Windows microphone indicator goes off; end-of-speech-to-first-sound latencies recorded for each. The same flow runs in CI with `--audio-source wav:`, the transcript stub and the stub harness.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. Slice S1 live passes on the owner's PC (recorded in the work package file with latencies, and with the `desktop.lock` hold), and its stub variant passes in CI; "what time is it" is answered through `SliceResolver` with no brain turn, and "stop" while Eileen speaks goes silent through the minimal core's barge-in.
  2. Killing a stand-in sidecar mid-call yields a typed error to the caller, an incident containing its hang dump from the guard, and a restart within the backoff bound, while the core keeps capturing.
  3. The `PreToolUse` endpoint answers no decision, `ask` and `deny` per tier and never `allow`; with the endpoint down a worker's guarded write is blocked; a held `approve` call (the permission-prompt tool, section 2.5) answers `allow` only after a `UserInput` approval and only with the input stored for its pending ask; the `PermissionRequest` tripwire answers `deny`.
  4. A process inside a worker Job Object holding every token file is refused by every loopback endpoint except its own session's hooks, and by every other role's dispatcher pipe; a shim started outside any Eileen job with a brain, worker, librarian or dev token is refused; a shim outside every Eileen job with the session token gets exactly the session toolset, and the same shim run inside a worker's Job Object with the session token is refused.
  5. Idle footprint of the thin release `eileen.exe`, measured with `cargo xtask measure-idle`, with stub sidecars and again with real WASAPI capture: under 60 MB working set; CPU within section 4.5's table; numbers recorded.
  6. `eileen trace last` stitches one turn across the core, a stub sidecar and a stub worker by `turn_` ID, every line with file and line; `eileen incident show last` summarises a captured incident; the end-to-end smoke asserts every log line inside a turn carries its ID.
  7. The redaction canary covers every sink this package writes (logs, incidents, share packs, recordings, the events table), including a spoken card number and an unaddressed utterance; a partial transcript of an utterance the gate then greys never reaches the events table, the loopback endpoint or a bus subscriber; `Record::PurgeJobs` leaves no row of the purged jobs or turns in `eileen.db` or its WAL, and no line in the logs when `logging.include_personal` is on.
  8. `eileen scenario save` then `share` then `submit --dry-run` produces a pull request body that passes `pr-meta`, a redacted folder that passes `scenarios check`, and a redacted copy that still replays in scenario mode; the saved and the shared scenario both carry the machine hash from `fleet\machine.toml`.
  9. The shuttle tests pass; fuzz targets `mcp_client_message`, `mcp_tool_arguments`, `dispatch_line`, `worker_hook_payload`, `http_request` and `scenario_file` run clean for 10 minutes each (`long.yml`) and their corpora replay on Windows.
  10. Path classification: table tests over real files and links in a temporary folder (an environment variable, `~`, a junction into a fake `.ssh` folder, an 8.3 short name, a `.lnk` to a `.ps1`, a UNC path, a glob, an `.html` file and a `.url` shortcut) give the classes and the `runs_code` and `opens_web` flags of section 2.12; with a temporary data folder, a job's worktree under `worktrees\` is `JobFolder` for that job and `EileenData` for another, its `.git` file's `gitdir` target and the common directory are `GitInternals`, and so are the worktree's own `.git` file, an in-place repository's `.git\hooks\pre-commit` and a nested `sub\.git\config` inside the job folder, as are, inside the job folder, `sub\.GIT\config`, `sub\.git.\config`, `sub\.git \hooks\x`, `sub\.git::$INDEX_ALLOCATION\config` and, for a path that does not exist yet, `sub\GIT~1\config`, and so are `alias\config` through a junction `alias` to `sub\.git`, a hard link in the job folder to `sub\.git\hooks\pre-commit`, `sub2\.git\config` where `sub2\.git` is a junction to a folder `plain` that holds a git directory, `plain\config` itself, the `\\?\`, `\??\`, `\\.\` and `\\localhost\C$` spellings of `sub\.git\config`, and the hashed short name `.git` gets in a folder where `git~1` to `git~4` exist; in shell commands, PowerShell's `sub\.gi[t]\config`, Git Bash's `sub/.gi{t,x}/config` and the fullwidth spelling of `sub\.git\config` are `Unknown`, and Git Bash's `/c/...` form of `sub\.git\config` is `GitInternals` ([2026-10-wp2-dotgit-spellings.md](research/2026-10-wp2-dotgit-spellings.md) measured these spellings and holds a probe script for them); an unrelated repository's `.git\config` elsewhere in the temporary folder is `Elsewhere` with `git_component` set, and another job's worktree's `.git` file is `EileenData`; with a fixture media history and ledger, a media-history URL gets a `UrlFact` with `in_index`, a URL from the ledger's browser lines gets `visited`, and the same host with another path or query gets neither; a git command run from `sub` gets a `GitFact` with `job_repo = false` and `has_hooks` from `sub\.git\hooks`, and one run from the job folder gets `job_repo = true`; the session's `run\<session>\plugin` is `SkillSnapshot`, its `run\<session>\cwd` is `JobFolder`, and `run\bus.json`, `run\session.json` and another session's folder are `EileenData`.
  11. `eileen scenario bless` rewrites a scenario's expected trace after a change that keeps every expectation passing, and refuses after a change that breaks one; `eileen scenario stale` writes today's date as `since`; an expired `stale` scenario, one whose `expires` is more than 30 days after its `since`, and one whose `since` is after today each fail `eileen scenario check` through `status_problems`, with the same results as check 18 on the same files.
  12. Machine-wide memory files, with `LOCALAPPDATA` pointed at a temporary folder: a runtime with a fake memory source runs for several sample periods and leaves nothing under `eileen\machine\` and no lock file; a runtime reading real memory through `SystemMemory` appends 5 s samples and one paging line per 30 s window, and a second such runtime started beside it adds no duplicate window; a runtime started with a fresh `EILEEN_DATA_DIR` reads the windows already in the machine-wide file and reports their limit in `MemoryPressure.paging_limit_kb_s`; a table test of `samples::auto_paging_limit` gives the learned value once unheld windows cover 24 hours, leaves `slot_held` windows out, falls back to the baseline's `slot0_paging_kb_s` with less than a day and to 5,000 KB/s with no `buildguard.toml`, and clamps to 2,000 and 40,000; on a virtual clock the limit is recomputed once an hour.
  13. Machine identity (owner answer 6): with a temporary data directory and a fake `MachineIdentity`, the first `eileen setup` creates the install id and `fleet\machine.toml`, a later `eileen run` keeps the install id and writes the same hash, a changed fake OS id changes the hash at the next start, and a fake with no OS id gives the install-id-only hash; on the owner's PC (`owner_pc::`) the `eileen-shell-win` implementation returns the MachineGuid, which `normalise_os_id` accepts, and no file Eileen writes contains the raw MachineGuid or the install id outside `fleet\machine.toml` (a grep over the data directory, the logs included).
  14. The machine profile's runtime (section 2.16), with `LOCALAPPDATA` pointed at a temporary folder: a runtime with the fake probe writes nothing under `eileen\machine\`; with the real probe it writes `facts.toml` at start, one `memory-hours.jsonl` line per hour on a virtual clock (a second runtime beside it adds none), and `plan.toml` on each `MachineCmd::WritePlan` (which the core emits with every `MachinePlanChanged`); a fake power-source change and a fake audio endpoint arrival each reach the core as `MachineEvent::Facts` within 1 s; the CPU busy figure from `GetSystemTimes` deltas passes its table test; `eileen doctor machine` prints the facts, the plan with its reasons and the last benchmarks with Eileen running and, from `plan.toml`, with it stopped. On the owner's PC (`owner_pc::`): the probe reads 16 GB, 16 physical cores and 22 logical processors with 6 in the highest efficiency class, the Arc GPU as an integrated adapter and the NPU as an NPU, the system volume as solid-state and the OS build, in under 200 ms, and no file under `machine\` contains a serial number, the machine's name or the user's name (a grep, as in acceptance 13).

### 6.5 WP3: Claude harness and the brain

- **Branch** `wp3/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A. **Desktop** no. **Wave** W1.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-harness`, `crates/eileen-stub-harness`, `tests/harness-scripts/`, `scenarios/claude/`, fuzz targets `stream_json_line` and `control_message` with their corpora and regressions, `docs/wp/WP3-harness.md`.
- **Builds.** First, on the owner's PC, the **approval contract test** against the real CLI (section 2.5): a tier-2 Bash call listed in `--allowedTools` with the guard answering `ask` (through a test hook listener in `eileen_harness::testkit`, since WP2's endpoint does not exist yet), tried through the `PermissionRequest` hook, `--permission-prompt-tool` (if the flag exists) and deny-then-steer; for each, allow runs the command, deny leaves no output file, a dead endpoint blocks it, a guard `deny` (tier 3) blocks a command that is listed in `--allowedTools`, a tool named in `--disallowedTools` cannot run, and a built-in tool not named in `--tools` does not exist; the subagent case (a `Task` call whose child runs the same command, with `Task` allowed only inside this test) is run and its result recorded for v0.2, since no v0.1 session gets subagent tools; the winning route, the permission mode and the exact reply shapes are recorded in the work package file and in a harness script, and the route goes into section 2.5 by amendment if it differs. The first pass of this test ran on 2.1.304 in `wp3/approval-spike` ([2026-10-wp3-approval-contract.md](research/2026-10-wp3-approval-contract.md)): the route is `--permission-prompt-tool mcp__eileen__approve` under `--permission-mode dontAsk`, now in section 2.5, and its case list and fixtures are the starting point for the harness script and `cargo xtask contract-claude`; when WP3 next changes the contracts it makes `HookRequest::PermissionRequest.tool_use_id` optional, because the CLI's `PermissionRequest` body carries none, and it accepts both `Task` and `Agent` as the subagent tool's name (`system/init` lists `Task`; the model calls `Agent`). In the same session, test `--restricted` per session kind (a coding worker that edits, builds and commits in its repository; a computer-use worker; the brain) and record whether it becomes the default, and **measure the other containment options for decision D40's v0.2 sandbox**: launching `claude.exe` in Windows Sandbox, in an AppContainer or less-privileged AppContainer, and under a restricted token at low integrity (does the CLI start, does sign-in hold, do the tools and hooks work, what does it cost in start time and memory), each recorded in the work package file. Then: a typed stream-json parser (line length capped before parsing; unknown `type` becomes `Unknown`; `system/init` recorded for feature detection; `Skill` tool_use becomes `SkillInvoked`; tool inputs redacted and capped at 2 KB; auth failures from the `result` event); `ClaudeWorker` with `SessionSpec` and `JobBinding` (the real `.exe` resolved through the npm shim or the native layout, never the desktop app's copy; spawned through `eileen_supervisor` at below-normal priority; the two environment policies of section 2.12; the per-session settings file with every hook and explicit timeouts; the generated MCP config with the shim and approved third-party servers, each call tagged with its server's rung; `--tools` per kind, `--disallowedTools` for the subagent tools, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--no-chrome`, `--no-session-persistence`, the pinned permission mode, the session's plugin snapshot directory, re-hashed against its manifest before spawn and checked against `system/init`; `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false` for every session, and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1` for every session whose `MemorySpec` writes are `Off`, which is every session until the memory gate below has passed and WP7 has flipped `learning.vendor_memory.claude` (D77); `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_CHECKPOINTING=1` and `fileCheckpointingEnabled: false` for every session; a build slot from `eileen-buildguard` for build-heavy repositories with `EILEEN_BUILD_SLOT_HELD`, the ticket's memory-scaled `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` and the shared sccache settings when sccache is installed; `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_JOB_MEMORY` when `memory.worker_job_limit_mb` is set, with the job notification turned into `WorkerEvent::MemoryLimit`; the observed text of outside reads kept for provenance; interrupt as a feature-detected `control_request`, cancel by killing the Job Object); the warm pool's session lifecycle (start, bind, re-arm, recycle); `BrainBackend` with `route()`, `brain_for` and `AnyBrain` (warm session, `--tools ""`, only `mcp__eileen__*`, the thinking-off measurement of section 2.3, hot spare, recycle with a summary, swap on crash or 30 s of silence, the harness component holding the Primary, Spare and Fallback slots, routing each `BrainCmd` by its slot, carrying out `Promote`, `Start` and `Stop`, and stamping every `BrainEvent` with its process's instance number, the optional speculative experiment; the other routes return `RouteUnavailable`); the brain benchmark (`brain_benchmark`, `eileen bench brain`, the `BrainBenchmark` settings check); model aliases resolved per CLI version into `run\models.json` (a one-turn probe per alias), full ids on every command line, the resolved id recorded beside every timing; the anticipation prompt line; git worktrees through the supervisor and in-place snapshot refs (`git stash create` plus HEAD under `refs/eileen/`) with restore; `continue_session` jobs (`--resume <id> --fork-session`); `eileen claude install|uninstall|update-window` and `eileen doctor claude` (`claude auth status`, the version range, installed against running versions, the toolchains a worker sees, never a credential file); the Codex app-server and ACP drivers as types returning `RouteUnavailable`, the mapping table in the crate doc, DeepSeek as configuration only; the flag constant the nightly drift job reads; `eileen-stub-harness` playing `StubScriptLine` scripts; the first scripts recorded from real sessions on the owner's PC and redacted with the `Redactor`; `cargo xtask contract-claude` (dispatched to a harness binary; owner's PC only: replays the scripts' prompts through the real CLI, compares event shapes, re-runs the approval cases and the skill-listing probe). The first pull request after the contract test that runs one worker job and one brain turn end to end (the parser, `ClaudeWorker` without the pool, `BrainBackend` without the spare) is `wp3/worker-brain`; slice S1 waits for it and not for the rest of WP3. **From D77** (section 2.10 item 15, after `wp3/worker-brain` and never blocking slice S1): `MemorySpec` with `Off` as the default; **the memory gate** on the owner's PC, on the pinned and the latest CLI with the exact flags of section 2.5 (the three poisoned and plain probes and the five questions of item 15's gate 1, with every change in the memory folders matched by path and hash to an approved write), recorded in the work package file before any kind runs with memory on; the `--restricted` probe with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=0` on the pinned and the latest CLI (never with a memory folder in `--add-dir`), with the trade recorded per kind; the probe of whether a worker under `dontAsk` can write into a pinned `autoMemoryDirectory` outside its working folders without `--add-dir`; the probe of whether a `continue_session` fork (`--resume <id> --fork-session`) runs with `--no-session-persistence`, which alone would let it carry note lines; the note-format probe writing `run\note-format.json` per CLI version; the check of the memory path in `system/init` before binding, restarting a mismatched session with memory off; the `PostToolUse` and `InstructionsLoaded` hooks and the hashes of the index and instruction files a session started with, for receipts and `UseRecord.env`; `--append-system-prompt-file` with the hub index for the brain and coding sessions, confirmed together with `--system-prompt`, and whether `--system-prompt-snapshot off` re-reads it; the brain prompt's dynamic boundary; the brain's time to first text with a full 200-line index; native compaction in a warm stream-json brain against a recycle; `--fallback-model` for workers; whether `--add-dir` with `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` loads a repository's own CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md when the folder is also the working folder; and `eileen doctor claude` requiring CLI 2.1.284 or later, with the nightly drift job checking that floor, the note frontmatter shape and `autoMemoryDirectory`. The owner confirms once, by listing folder names by hand and opening no file, that the CLI's file-history folder holds nothing for an Eileen `--session-id`; Eileen's code never looks there.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. The approval contract test passes on the owner's PC with the chosen route recorded, including the guard deny of an allowed command, the disallowed and unnamed tools, and the dead endpoint; the subagent case's result is recorded, and subagent tools stay disallowed in v0.1 whatever it shows. The containment measurements (Windows Sandbox, AppContainer, restricted token) are recorded.
  2. Against `eileen-stub-harness` in CI: a worker job runs to completion through a pooled session bind; `> ` narration lines become `JobNarration`; an injected permission request reaches the approval path; a `Skill` call becomes `SkillInvoked`; a crash marks the job failed with a reason; cancel leaves no child process (nextest leak detection).
  3. On the owner's PC (`real_claude::`): the brain reaches `system/init` with connectors off and only the `eileen` server listed (and a worker's init lists only `eileen` plus approved servers); warm-turn time to first text over ten turns on a quiet machine, with each thinking-off method, recorded with the resolved model id; worker cold start recorded on a quiet PC and under typical load, pool bind time recorded, and whether the pool fitted under typical load (and by which admission rule) recorded, with available memory, commit headroom, the paging rate and the `auto` paging limit beside it. The admission part of this record is a W2 item, because WP3's harness does not contain the admission rules: it is taken with the full runtime (`eileen run`) once WP1's admission rules and WP2's memory sampler have merged and the machine-wide `paging-30s.jsonl` holds at least a day, and it passes only if, on a typical working day with the owner's usual apps and several agent sessions, a cold worker was admitted by rule 2 or 3 within 10 minutes without "start it anyway" (otherwise an amendment sets the admission defaults from the record before WP7 and WP9 start); a mid-turn `control_request` interrupt verified; learning-loop row 7 reproduced with this package's exact flags (a worker uses a learned test skill from its snapshot).
  4. The worker environment holds no variable on the denylist and keeps the pass-through list; the brain's holds only the allowlist; `cargo --version` resolves in a coding worker's shell in the stub run; no code path opens a path under the user's `.claude` folder (check 14 plus a file-access check in the stub run; D77 keeps this as written unless the owner approves its proposed allowlist, which only WP7's `eileen_learn::vendor_notes` would use). On the owner's PC (`real_claude::`, under a build slot): a coding worker in a scratch Rust repository and one in a scratch Node repository each run `cargo test` and `npm test` green from their own shell, which proves MSVC discovery (vswhere and `ProgramFiles`), linking and Node resolution in the worker environment; recorded in the work package file.
  5. `eileen doctor claude` reports a signed-out CLI clearly and suggests the user run `claude auth login`, without running it; it names the npm shim it resolved and the real executable behind it.
  6. The default config names models only through `routing.models` aliases; the harness resolves each to a full id per CLI version (cached in `run\models.json`, re-resolved when the CLI version changes), every command line carries a full id, and every recorded timing carries the resolved id. `eileen bench brain` produces the B10 numbers for Claude; `brain_for` returns `RouteUnavailable` for every other route; the Claude backend and driver report `LanguageSupport::Any` through `languages()`; a stub session that allocates past a small `worker_job_limit_mb` fails its job with the limit named.
  7. Fuzz target `stream_json_line` runs clean for 10 minutes (`long.yml`); its corpus replays on Windows.
  8. Notes (D77): against the stub harness, every session spawns with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1` and file checkpointing off while `MemorySpec` is `Off`, a native-writing session carries `autoMemoryDirectory` naming its folder, and a session whose `system/init` reports another memory path is never bound and is restarted with memory off; the brain and a coding session carry the hub index through `--append-system-prompt-file`, and `continue_session` carries no note lines unless the fork probe showed it runs with `--no-session-persistence`. On the owner's PC (`real_claude::`): the memory gate's record, the `--restricted` record per kind, the `continue_session` fork probe, `run\note-format.json`, the brain's time to first text with a 200-line index, the compaction measurement and the `--add-dir` instruction-file result are each recorded in the work package file, and the gate's record names every change in the memory folders and the approved write it matched (a change that matched none fails the gate and keeps native writing off).

### 6.6 WP4: Voice (audio, speech, dictation, voices, the first-run recording)

- **Branch** `wp4/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A. **Desktop** yes (audio devices). **Wave** W1 for its two slice pull requests, W2 for the rest (which starts when both have merged). Speech-model tests and benchmarks take the exclusive build slot, and device tests take `desktop.lock` (never during WP6's spike, rule 12). Its first two pull requests are slice S1's and come before everything else: `wp4/s1-audio` (capture, render and loopback, AEC, mute, the ring buffer, playback accounting, the energy barge-in fallback) and `wp4/s1-speech` (the pipe, VAD, Nemotron, end of turn, Kokoro with its front end, barge-in windows, the transcript-injection stub).
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-audio`, `crates/eileen-audio-win`, `crates/eileen-speech` (including `models.toml`), `crates/eileen-dictation`, `scenarios/voice/`, `scenarios/dictation/`, fuzz targets `speech_frame` and `dictation_cleanup` with their corpora and regressions, `docs/wp/WP4-voice.md`.
- **Builds.** `eileen-audio`: cpal WASAPI capture, render and loopback at 48 kHz with 10 ms buffers (cpal only under the Windows target table; the logic around it neutral); resampling (pick `rubato` or an own polyphase filter by measurement); sonora 0.2.0 AEC3 with noise suppression and high-pass, the loopback mix as the far-end reference and Eileen's PCM as the fallback, bypassed after 500 ms of loopback silence; the other-audio signal; 512-sample 16 kHz frames into an SPSC ring buffer (loom-tested under `cfg(loom)`) and the speech pipe; mute that stops the capture stream; duck, pause, resume and stop with samples-played accounting; PCM playback; `AudioSource` and `AudioSink` with `WavSource`, `CaptureSink` and `RoomMixer` (with a music or speech-video bed); the audio heartbeat and tick ring (no tracing and no allocation in the callback, checked by a counting allocator in debug builds); the energy barge-in fallback; short recordings for enrollment and the first run. `eileen-audio-win`: endpoints, loopback, the Communications category option with Windows ducking opted out, MMCSS (system volume moved to `eileen-desktop-win`'s `SystemVolume`). `eileen-speech` (lib and bin): the pipe protocol of section 3.3.7 with `source`, length validation before allocation, and TraceCtx in JSON; `crates/eileen-speech/tools/build-sherpa-native.ps1`, the script WP0's `native.yml` workflow runs, which builds sherpa-onnx 1.13.8 shared MT without espeak-ng and piper-phonemize, checks the output for espeak-ng symbols and data, and publishes it as a release asset with its SHA256 for `fetch-native` (the new pin goes into xtask's native table by a one-line amendment); the plug-in traits `VoiceActivity`, `Recognizer`, `TurnDetector` and `Synth`, each reporting its languages (a `LanguageSupport` read from its model's `models.toml` entry, which records the publisher's language tags) and measured cost, with the engine per stage chosen by `speech.vad`, `speech.asr` and `speech.turn`; Silero VAD v6.2 through sherpa-onnx; Nemotron streaming int8 fed only during speech, with confidence; turn IDs at speech onset; each turn's active speech level in `EndOfTurn.level_db`; Smart Turn v3.2 through `ort` =2.0.0-rc.13 (with `copy-dylibs`) and the Rust log-mel, fused with continuation cues and the stretched cap; Kokoro through `ort` with Eileen's espeak-free front end (the CMUdict lexicon and a small permissively licensed ONNX G2P for unknown words, mapped to Kokoro's phonemes), with clause chunking, a short first clause, synthesis ahead of playback, pause and resume, and the phrase cache (stock and per-app launch phrases); barge-in windows with the stricter threshold while other audio plays; `Synth` implementations for Kokoro, Pocket TTS (through the espeak-free sherpa-onnx build, or `ort` on the same graphs if that build has no TTS) and Chatterbox (Turbo from the official ONNX graphs in q4 or q8, Nano from a community export once licence and digest check out; no PyTorch, no Python), the engine per voice read from `voice.engines.<name>`, the `VoiceRealtime` check for the settings flow, the per-reply watchdog and whole-line rendering above 0.9; `eileen-speech voice render` with the opt-in AudioSeal stage and `check-mark`, AudioSeal's generator and detector exported once to ONNX by a pinned script under `crates/eileen-speech/tools/` and published as a release asset; a draft of the Chatterbox-Nano licence question of owner answer 14, posted to its maintainer only after the owner's go-ahead (the Pocket TTS question was dropped by the owner's direction of 2026-10-25); CAM++ for enrollment and the speaker check, used for gating only if it clears the bar of section 2.8 (acceptance 13), with the `SpeakerBar` it writes to `models.toml` and `VoiceCalibration.speaker_enrolled`; consent enrollment, dropped clips with consent, voice deletion; the first-run calibration of section 2.8 (echo delay and enhancement measured while Eileen's own prompt plays, mic level and VAD offset from the clip, the name variant from the greeting's transcript, the recognizer's mean confidence against the threshold in `models.toml` with the Parakeet comparison when it is low, the speech-level reference for the addressee score, measured exactly like every turn's `EndOfTurn.level_db`, the speaker enrollment if adopted, the clone reference) written to `calibration.toml` and sent to the core as `VoiceEvent::Calibration` (and `Calibration(None)` after "forget my voice"), the clone-timing measurement in `eileen setup` on a bundled synthetic reference clip, "forget my voice", and the clone-timing check; the name-recognisability check; the two embedding candidates of section 3.3.10 (the MiniLM-class transformer with an own WordPiece tokenizer, and the static-embedding model in plain Rust), measured on the paraphrase cases and the 20 ms budget, the better one adopted; hotwords (only if beam search measures acceptable); Parakeet v3 on demand with idle unload; the plan's small models (Kroko, Kokoro int8) and the plan's `speech.threads` and `speech.tts_precision`; the machine profile's speech part (section 2.16): `eileen-speech.exe bench` and the setup benchmarks (`Vad`, `Asr`, `Tts`, `CloneTtfa`, `Embed`), each with its conditions, reported as `VoiceEvent::Bench`, the execution providers the shipped ONNX Runtime can create, reported as `VoiceEvent::Providers` at start, a command corpus (the grammar's phrases and the resolver golden file's commands) and each recognizer's command accuracy on it written to `models.toml` (`command_accuracy_per_mille`, which row 1 of the plan reads), and `AudioEndpoints::endpoints` and `watch_endpoints` in `eileen-audio-win` through `IMMNotificationClient`; the start-up check of the loaded `onnxruntime.dll`; EcoQoS opt-out; `models.toml` (URL, size, full SHA256, license, residency) and the speech part of `eileen setup`; `eileen-speech voice list|learn|use|delete|render|check-mark` and `eileen-speech setup` (which `eileen voice` and `eileen setup` run); the transcript-injection stub mode (`eileen-speech --stub <file>`). `eileen-dictation`: tier-0 cleanup implementing `DictationCleaner`, SpeakoFlow's 79 conformance cases (MIT, attributed) and Eileen's typed-slot positives and negatives.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. Synthetic echo through `RoomMixer`, with Eileen speaking and with a music bed and a speech-video bed: zero far-end words leak into the transcript, the user's command is fully recovered, and barge-in false triggers stay under 1 per 10 minutes of video.
  2. Barge-in on the owner's PC with speakers: speech onset to silence at most 150 ms at p95 over 20 trials, with the correct heard prefix; a backchannel resumes.
  3. Idle listening: the speech process under 8 percent of one core and the full stack about 1.25 GB resident; the core's idle CPU with AEC bypassed and running, and the loopback capture's own cost; recorded with the machine state.
  4. End of turn: half-finished-sentence cases do not end early on the synthetic set (and on the owner's consented local recordings once he records them, never committed; the gap is noted until then).
  5. Measured with the exclusive slot and recorded: Kokoro first clause and real-time factor through the shipped sherpa-onnx path on a quiet PC; Kokoro int8 against fp32 under equal load; Parakeet Unified EN against Nemotron (WER, partial latency, tail, RSS); the embedding model's round trip for a 12-word utterance against the 20 ms p95 budget; the first command after 30 idle minutes with Chrome, VS Code and Resolve open, with and without `pin_working_set`.
  6. The owner's head-to-head, with at least 6 GB free: Pocket TTS against Chatterbox-Nano and Chatterbox-Turbo, each cloning the owner's own voice (consented, never committed), with time to first audio, real-time factor at 2, 4 and 8 threads, resident memory and a listening test; "use Chatterbox for <voice>" and `eileen voice use <voice> --engine chatterbox-turbo` go through the settings flow with the `VoiceRealtime` check, say whether the engine will speak live, and "undo that" reverts; the watchdog's live or announcements-only decision follows the numbers.
  7. Voice enrollment refuses a consent clip from a different speaker (committed synthetic WAV fixtures); a dropped clip without its owner's consent recording is refused; deleting a voice, and declining the first-run reveal, remove every file (`NoFileLeft`).
  8. Dictation cleanup passes all 79 SpeakoFlow conformance cases and Eileen's negatives ("I actually enjoyed the movie" untouched); fuzz targets `dictation_cleanup` and `speech_frame` clean for 10 minutes each (`long.yml`).
  9. Mute stops capture within one buffer and the Windows microphone-in-use indicator goes off (manual check recorded); `eileen-speech` exits with a clear error when the wrong `onnxruntime.dll` is loaded.
  10. The transcript-injection stub lets CI run end-to-end scenarios with no models and no audio device, and the neutral speech and audio crates' tests pass on `ubuntu-latest`.
  11. No watermark by default: a test asserts the synthesis pipeline of every engine has no watermark stage with default settings, and the AudioSeal detector finds no mark in a default `voice render` file; with `voice.watermark_exports` on, it finds the mark in a rendered file and in a recording's audio lane. The release lane's licence listing shows no GPL component, and `native.yml`'s archive contains no espeak-ng symbol or data file.
  12. The first-run calibration on the owner's PC records echo delay and enhancement from Eileen's own prompt, the mic level, the recognizer's confidence, the speech-level reference for the addressee score (sent to the core as `VoiceEvent::Calibration`; a committed synthetic clip played 12 dB quieter gives an `EndOfTurn.level_db` 12 dB lower, within 2 dB) and, when the recognizer mishears "Eileen" in a recorded greeting, a name variant that then makes the name match; the confidence threshold is measured on WP4's corpus and written to `models.toml`, and a committed low-confidence synthetic clip triggers the offer of the other English model with both confidences recorded; declining the reveal, or missing the 40 s render limit, leaves `calibration.toml` and no audio, and "forget my voice" removes it (`NoFileLeft`).
  13. The speaker check against the bar of section 2.8, on the owner's PC with the exclusive slot, recorded as a `SpeakerBar` in `models.toml` and in the work package file, with the audio never committed: CAM++ (and any candidate embedder behind the same trait) scores command-length utterances (one to three seconds: commands, replies and action words such as "yes, push") from the owner's consented set (owner answer 11) in four room conditions (at the desk in quiet; at the desk while the PC plays music and then a video through its speakers, with echo cancellation on; with another person talking in the room; from across the room), and the same kinds of utterances from at least five other people who recorded their consent, in the same conditions. The threshold is chosen on one half and the rates are reported on the other, which holds at least 300 owner utterances and at least 300 utterances by other people; the check passes only if both one-sided 95 percent Clopper-Pearson upper bounds are under 1 percent. Synthetic voices (Kokoro's stock voices, a cloned voice of someone else) are scored and reported separately and never count toward the bar. When the check does not pass, or fewer than five other people or 300 utterances in either set are available, `passed = false` is written with the numbers, and a test runs every gating rule with that record and finds no utterance or approval refused for voice; enrollment's same-speaker comparison still refuses a mismatched consent clip (acceptance 7). A name keyword spotter, if evaluated, is recorded the same way.
  14. The machine profile's speech part (section 2.16), measured with the exclusive slot and recorded: the setup benchmarks run in under 60 s beyond model loading on the owner's PC and reach the core as `MachineEvent::Bench` with their conditions; `Asr` and `Tts` run at 2 and 4 threads, and the thread count the plan picks from them never exceeds the performance cores; the command accuracy of Nemotron and of Kroko on the command corpus (synthesized, and from the owner's consented set once he has recorded it) is written to `models.toml`, which settles whether Kroko may ever replace Nemotron; a recognizer swap the plan orders at a quiet moment unloads one model and loads the other while the core's ring buffer keeps the audio, and a command spoken during the swap is recognised after it (recorded); `AudioEndpoints::endpoints` lists the owner's microphone array and speakers with form factor and bus, a USB or Bluetooth headset arriving raises a change within 1 s, and endpoint names leave the process only as `Personal`; `VoiceEvent::Providers` lists the CPU provider of the shipped runtime.

### 6.7 WP5: Desktop control

- **Branch** `wp5/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A, WP1, WP2 (and so on slice S1 having passed). **Desktop** yes. **Wave** W3.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-desktop`, `crates/eileen-desktop-win` (including the `testpad` fixture as an example binary), `scenarios/desktop/`, `docs/wp/WP5-desktop.md`.
- **Builds.** Port the computer-use proof (`%USERPROFILE%\Projects\eileen-workfiles\2026-10-scratch\computer-use\proof\` on the owner's PC; bump rmcp to the workspace's 3.6.0) into the neutral server (`eileen-desktop`: the 18 tool handlers and their `ToolSpec`s with per-op tiers, `observes` rules (`ByTarget` for `desktop_tree`, `desktop_state`, `desktop_windows`, `desktop_wait_for` and every action whose verification hint reads a value, name or title back) and output schemas, the safety rules of section 2.6 including the target-category tiers from `app-categories.toml` (process names, AUMIDs, browser hosts and title suffixes, the recorder category), Eileen's own hotkeys as blocked combos, the send-like word list with localized forms, typed-text redaction and Luhn, per-app grants, element indexing and selector identity, evidence-based re-finding for self-healing, DPI mapping math and the mapping line, set-of-mark drawing, the app-name scorer with the margin rule, the insertion ladder's decisions, the manifest and app capabilities, `sanitize`, screenshots as text plus image only) and the Windows backend (`eileen-desktop-win`: per-monitor-v2 first; UIA cache requests on a dedicated MTA thread with deadlines, abandoned and replaced on a hang, the process killed only after three abandoned threads; window management and launch on their own thread; `SetWinEventHook` and on-demand UIA (no global focus handler for Chromium and Electron) feeding `DesktopDelta`s and the DesktopState cache; Raw Input for physical activity; monitors with stable ordinals; DWM frame bounds; executable versions; `BitBlt` and `PrintWindow` capture, resize to 1280 px and JPEG q80; `SendInput` one event at a time with the foreground and physical-input guard, leases, cursor restore only if unmoved; `ElementFromPoint` and `WindowFromPoint` checks; Invoke with a foreground check before and after; the `shell:AppsFolder` index persisted to `cache\apps.json` and refreshed in the background, AUMID launch through `eileen_supervisor::user_launch` that waits for the main window; moves and snaps against work areas; `Show` with the idle check, the spoken line from its reason kind and `Back`; the lease's spoken notice, countdown and hand-back line; `WM_CLOSE` through `SendMessageTimeout` with an unsaved-state check; system volume through `SystemVolume` (IAudioEndpointVolume); the web content tree of one Chromium window on demand as the `Dom` rung behind `desktop.web_tree`; the browser page's address-bar URL over UIA (its host in `WindowInfo.url_host`, unknown when the bar cannot be read; the full URL only in the focus change the ledger stores, never for a private window); the `test-physical` feature with its named-pipe injector, never enabled in a shipped binary; bounded cross-process calls; `EM_REPLACESEL`; the port of Handy's clipboard transaction (MIT, attribution in `NOTICE`) with terminal-class detection for Ctrl+Shift+V; paced Unicode `SendInput` with read-back; TextPattern selection and the clipboard fallback with the exclusion formats; presentation state; microphone-in-use detection). `DesktopIntent` with the configurable wait and cancel. Fix the testpad fixture so its controls stay inside its window at any DPI. EcoQoS opt-out at start.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. On owned fixtures (testpad, Notepad launched by AUMID and closed unsaved, an isolated Edge profile; on the hosted lane the classic Notepad started at medium by acceptance 9's helper instead of launched by AUMID): background Invoke and SetValue verified by the fixture's own state; a close after a change made only through `desktop_set_value`, on the classic and the packaged Notepad, is caught by the unsaved-state check itself rather than by the app's prompt, which may not come (acceptance 9); password fields refused (also through `ElementFromPoint` for a coordinate click); every blocked combo refused; a coordinate click that would land on another window or on an Eileen window refused; a foreground action with the user idle succeeds after the spoken notice and countdown and restores the previous foreground with the hand-back line; with the `test-physical` injector standing in for the user, moving the mouse during a lease, and separately typing during a synthetic sequence, each stop the sequence within one event, and the cursor stays where the "user" put it; the owner repeats both cases once by hand with the real mouse and keyboard (the Raw Input path itself) and the result is recorded in the work package file; when Windows refuses the foreground, it says so and never loops; an Invoke that moves the foreground on a Win32 button is detected and handled.
  2. `desktop_state` p95 under 100 ms on the owner's desktop from the cache; `desktop_tree` for Notepad and Explorer within the proof's numbers; recorded.
  3. A deliberately hung UIA provider fixture makes the call fail with a typed error within its deadline, the thread is abandoned and replaced, "open Notepad" and a window snap still work during the hang, and only repeated hangs restart the sidecar, with the core listening throughout.
  4. A real Claude worker on the owner's PC (`real_claude::`) opens Notepad, writes a sentence with `desktop_set_value` and reads it back through the shim and dispatcher, after the per-app consent question; the trajectory has every step with a verification hint and a rung.
  5. Dictation insertion is exact in a RichEdit fixture via `EM_REPLACESEL` and in Notepad and an isolated Edge text area via the clipboard transaction, with the user's clipboard restored (a full-format snapshot test with an image on the clipboard); a clipboard entry marked for exclusion is never read into DesktopState.
  6. The Chromium probe is recorded: time for web content to appear over UIA once asked, whether a page password field is detected through `ElementFromPoint`, and Chrome's private bytes and CPU with Eileen running against not running.
  7. `scenarios/desktop/` covers open app (including the ambiguous video-editor case), snap to a half, move to a monitor (`monitors>=2`), close, volume, what am I looking at, and insert text; they replay in simulation and run in the desktop lane (on the hosted lane, those that open an app through the sidecar and then drive it declare `medium-shell` in `requires`, WP9 acceptance 10).
  8. DPI mapping unit tests for 100, 125, 150 and 200 percent and for monitors left of and above the primary (fakes); the testpad fixture keeps its controls inside its window at 144 DPI.
  9. Hosted runners give UIA an interactive desktop (settled, section 7, [2026-10-hosted-runner-desktop.md](research/2026-10-hosted-runner-desktop.md)). The hosted session is elevated (why is unverified), and so is its Explorer, so anything `user_launch` starts through the shell there runs at high integrity, and whether a medium caller can use that elevated shell at all was not measured (unverified). For that lane WP5 builds a test helper that lowers the runner's token to start the sidecar, and every app a scenario drives, at medium integrity, and hands each app to the scenario by window handle; nothing started that way compiles code at run time (`Add-Type`, which compiles through `csc.exe`, failed under such a token with `ERROR_PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD`; the failing call is unverified). No test process, helper or script loads the .NET UI Automation client and then asserts on Win32 or WinForms controls. The nightly's first desktop job is a canary that keeps the answer true, recording `ImageOS`, `ImageVersion`, the UAC values and the RID of the runner's account: from the test process, inside the runner's job, a COM round trip in the classic Notepad and an Invoke on the testpad; through the helper at medium, the sidecar setting and reading back a value in a classic Notepad started at medium, the testpad and the isolated Edge profile started at medium with a medium-to-medium Invoke on the testpad and a ValuePattern round trip in Edge's page, and `eileen run` started at medium with every sidecar it spawns at medium; a medium sidecar's `user_launch` of the classic Notepad, recording the launched app's integrity, its job and any error; and the sidecar's SetValue and typing into an elevated classic Notepad (its Edit reached by window handle, since from medium its tree shows only the title bar) and Invoke on an elevated testpad's button, each reported as refused by read-back, never as done (Windows returns success for all three). A failure fails the job with the image version, and desktop tests run only in the owner and fleet lanes until it is understood; until the Edge cases pass, Edge scenarios stay off the hosted lane. The x64 hosted images carry the classic Notepad and Windows 11 the packaged one, so the Notepad cases of acceptance items 1 and 5 run on both, the hosted lane covering the classic Notepad and the owner's PC the packaged one; item 4 runs on the owner's PC's Notepad and records which one it was. The unsaved-state check handles a close that brings no prompt: on the hosted ARM image the packaged Notepad closed without one in runs 2, 3 and 5, where its text was set only through ValuePattern, and prompted in run 6, where text was also typed, so whether a SetValue-only change counts as unsaved is unverified, and acceptance 1's SetValue-only close covers it.
  10. Neutral table tests of the tier decisions: Enter and a send-like click in a fixture window whose process is in the mail category, and Enter in a browser window whose title marks a chat site, are tier 2; `Shift+Delete` is tier 2; pressing any of Eileen's configured hotkeys, including the approve chord, is refused; typed text holding a Luhn-valid card number or a secret-like token is tier 3, and the Redactor's patterns are tested on the same path; `Show` while the user is typing is refused and the result stays on the HUD.
  11. On the testpad and an isolated Edge profile: the web content tree of a fixture page serves `desktop_tree` with rung `Dom`, a click on one of its elements needs no screenshot, and the cost against the Chromium probe's baseline is recorded; that tree read is classed `Web` by `observed_source`, and the testpad's tree `Tree`.
  12.
