Apps Eileen launches leave its jobs (section 2.1): Notepad launched by `desktop_launch_app` and a fixture "host app" launched by `apps_open_path` are in no Eileen Job Object, carry none of Eileen's environment variables and keep running when the desktop and apps sidecars are restarted; the fixture host app then starts `eileen-shim --role session` from the install folder, and the dispatcher accepts it for the session role (the case of the owner opening the Claude app by voice), while the same shim started from inside a worker's job is refused. On the hosted lane only the shell's route is tested, and only for job membership, environment and survival, recording the launched app's integrity (high there, because the hosted shell is elevated); the shim half runs on the owner's PC, and so does the breakaway fallback, because the runner's own job allows no breakaway (`CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` fails with error 5 there).
  13. The address bar (section 7, "Carried into packages"): on an isolated Edge profile, and on Chrome when it is installed, a fixture page loaded with a path, a query and a fragment gives `WindowInfo.url_host` equal to its host and a focus-change URL equal to the URL loaded (the scheme restored when the bar hides it), in a normal and in a private window (whose URL reaches no store); a window whose bar cannot be read reports its host as unknown; the work package file records which browsers and settings shorten or hide the path or query, and for those the URL rule's rule 3 stays fail-closed.

### 6.8 WP6: App adapters

- **Branch** `wp6/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A, WP1, WP2; step 0 depends on nothing. **Desktop** yes. **Wave** step 0 in W1, the rest in W4.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-apps` (including `models.toml`), `crates/eileen-apps-win`, `adapters/resolve/`, `scenarios/apps/`, fuzz targets `resolve_reply` and `registry_reply` with their corpora and regressions, `docs/wp/WP6-apps.md`.
- **Step 0, the correction-route spike** (W1, on the owner's PC, no Rust compiling, with his agreement (owner answer 9) to launch Resolve and create one dedicated test project, under the exclusive build slot while Resolve runs): with the Lua bridge from the app-hooks report (and the `hookprobe` binary already built in `%USERPROFILE%\Projects\eileen-workfiles\2026-10-scratch\app-hooks\hookprobe`), test in order: importing a generated Fusion `.setting` with an ellipse mask keyframed from boxes and a sharpen node through `ImportFusionComp` (and whether it renders); a DRX that carries a window at a given rectangle; undo per route (deleting an imported composition by name and loading the previous current one with `DeleteFusionCompByName` and `LoadFusionCompByName`, and checking that loading a color version does not remove a composition; `AddVersion` with `LoadVersionByName` for the DRX route); `GetRenderJobStatus` progress; the cost of exporting stills every N frames across a clip in one bridge call (`export_stills`) for tracking; whether `ApplyGradeFromDRX` replaces existing nodes. Record the results and the chosen route in `docs/wp/WP6-apps.md`, and open an amendment to section 3.3.9 if `ResolveOp` needs to change.
- **Builds** (W4). In `eileen-apps` (neutral, tested on Linux): the tool handlers and `ToolSpec`s with per-op tiers, read-only flags, idempotence, inverses, output schemas, `observes` rules (`routes_lookup` and `apps_media` `status` are `Web`, `apps_recent` is `ByArgs`, and the user's own Resolve project and timeline reads are listed in `contracts/observes-none.toml`) and `preview_before`; `apps_open_path`'s tier from the runtime's `PathFact`s (`runs_code`, the class); `routes_lookup` (the registry query through `curl.exe` started by `eileen_supervisor`, one fixed host, a 10 s deadline, a 256 KB cap, a day's cache, the fuzzed `registry_reply` parser, registry ids, packages, status and verified namespaces in the reply, `from_app_vendor` from `app-vendors.toml`, which this package writes and maintains); the record of each approved server's tool names in `cache\mcp-tools.json` (one `tools/list` run through the supervisor right after each install, the sidecar being the file's only writer); the installer for approved MCP servers (npm with `--ignore-scripts` and PyPI through `uv` at the exact registry version into `mcp\<app>\`, an app-bundled executable by its confirmed path, refusals for install scripts, OCI images and remote-only entries, the recorded digest); the render discovery poll (`is_rendering` every 5 s while the bridge is up, at once on request) and `RenderStarted`, `RenderProgress` and `RenderDone` events; the ResolveMCP client path (when `ResolveMCP.exe` exists, each op served through it where it covers the op and through the bridge otherwise, the rung recorded per step); drop-frame-aware absolute timecode math; the bridge line protocol parser (parse JSON, never compare strings); the YouTube URL builder with `&t=`; SMTC live position; the media history; face picking, crops, interpolated tracking from per-frame boxes, the variance-of-Laplacian metric; the Fusion `.setting` generator (and the DRX generator if the spike says it works); recent-work ranking with caller-supplied focus-time boosts and thumbnails for contact sheets; the manifest and app capabilities with real versions. In `eileen-apps-win` (unsafe only in its `ffi` module): `AppHost` implementations for Word, Excel, Explorer, Photoshop and the Resolve process gate; one SMTC manager for the process lifetime with the four subscriptions and control confirmed by the next event; URLs, allowlisted URIs and paths opened through `eileen_supervisor::user_launch` (the shell's own `ShellExecute`, never inside an Eileen job), with the wait for the expected media title and the `reuse_playing` seek; the late-bound `IDispatch` helper on per-target STA threads with call timeouts and an `IMessageFilter`, attaching through the running object table with `CLSCTX_LOCAL_SERVER`, for Word, Excel, Explorer (tabs) and Photoshop (tested against a fake COM target only); the recent-work index (jump lists, `.lnk` bytes, Office MRU, a `ReadDirectoryChangesW` watcher, the media history). Resolve: the bridge in `adapters/resolve/eileen_resolve_bridge.lua` with every method of section 2.7, spawned once through `eileen_supervisor` and gated on `Resolve.exe`, readiness polling after a launch, version reporting, render progress events for app tasks. YuNet (OpenCV Zoo, MIT) through `ort` against the shipped DLL, pinned in `models.toml`. `eileen-apps doctor resolve [--route mcp|bridge]` (which `eileen doctor resolve` runs; `--route` sends every check through one Resolve route). EcoQoS opt-out at start. Playwright MCP stays optional and documented, not built.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. On the dedicated test project in Resolve Studio 21.0.1: `goto` 15:30 lands on the computed timecode for 24, 25, 29.97 drop-frame and 30 fps timelines starting at 01:00:00:00, after opening the Edit page; `export_still` writes a frame and `export_stills` writes every Nth frame of the clip; the chosen route's edit and its journaled inverse restore the clip (the Fusion route: Eileen's composition deleted and the previous one current again; the DRX route: the previous version loaded); `SetCurrentTimecode`, `GetCurrentVideoItem`, `ExportCurrentFrameAsStill` and the edit's latencies are recorded; a render started through `render_start` reports progress as an app task. Once the owner is on Resolve 21.1 or later (owner answer 9), the same checks pass through ResolveMCP for every op it covers and again with `doctor resolve --route bridge`, so the Lua bridge stays a proven fallback.
  2. With Resolve closed no call stalls: the gate answers that Resolve is not running in under 50 ms; launching it, `hello` is polled until ready and the project and timeline questions follow the flow of E7.
  3. YuNet finds the faces in a committed synthetic test image (generated, no real person), picks left and right correctly, returns a crop, tracks a moving synthetic face across stills, and sharpness rises after a known sharpen filter.
  4. SMTC pause, play and seek on a silent-WAV test media session, each confirmed by the next event; on the owner's PC a YouTube URL with `&t=200` plays from 3:20, a seek to 60 s through `apps_media` is confirmed once the session returns, and "play that video at 1:10" resolves through the media history with no model; `apps_open_url` with `reuse_playing` on a session already showing the expected title seeks it and opens no second tab or window (`reused` true).
  5. Word selection and Excel active cell read from documents the test creates and closes without saving; `apps_open_path` opens a fixture file; `apps_open_uri` refuses a scheme outside the allowlist.
  6. Fuzz target `resolve_reply` clean for 10 minutes (`long.yml`).
  7. `scenarios/apps/` covers Resolve goto, the crop confirmation, undo on the chosen route, render progress for a render started by voice and for one started in Resolve's own window (discovered by the poll), YouTube play-at and seek, pause and play, Word selection; B1's deterministic phases run end to end with a stub worker for any delegated step.
  8. `routes_lookup` parses a recorded registry reply into `Untrusted` server entries with registry ids, packages, status and the namespace of each server name, and never installs anything; against `app-vendors.toml`, a recorded entry `io.github.someone/resolve-mcp` gets `from_app_vendor` false and the display "GitHub user someone", one in `com.blackmagicdesign` gets true and "blackmagicdesign.com", and no field is taken from the registry's `_meta` "official" block; `registry_reply` fuzzing is clean for 10 minutes; after an approved `mcp.servers` change, the installer puts a fixture npm package at its exact version into the app's folder with `--ignore-scripts`, records its digest, and the generated MCP config launches it from there (no `npx`); a package that needs install scripts, an OCI image and a remote-only entry are refused with a sentence; with ResolveMCP present (once the owner is on 21.1 or later), the map of ops it covers is recorded and each covered op's steps carry rung `AppMcp`.

- **Branch** `wp7/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A, WP1, WP2, WP3. **Desktop** no. **Wave** phase A in W3 (with WP5), phase B in W4 (with WP6).

- **Phase A builds** (W3). Its first pull request wires `eileen_intent::Resolver` into the composition root in place of WP2's `SliceResolver` and deletes it (an amendment to WP2's files), with slice S1's stub variant still green. `eileen-intent` (pure, implements `IntentResolver`): the grammar of section 2.4 as data with its precedence, slot resolvers including `Media` and `Ambiguous` results, normalisation, the content-word rule, learned templates (port the matcher from `%USERPROFILE%\Projects\eileen-workfiles\2026-10-scratch\learning\matcher\`), BM25 hints with an absolute floor, depth overrides, the fact phrases producing `CoreCommand::Fact(FactCommand)` with the statements-only bounds applied to slot text, the subject key, the feedback scope from `follow_up`, the complaint lexicon and `fact-subjects.toml` (section 2.10 item 11), `follow_up` over `ResolveCtx.last_turn` (pronouns and ellipses bound by the slot resolvers to the last turn's items, job, apps, files and media, else a shared content word or topic; for the machine profile (section 2.16), the fitted keys as `auto` values with their `SettingSpec` entries (risk `Misfire(MachineFit)`, never `Safety`; applied live, or by restarting the speech models for rows 1, 2 and 6), the `machine` section, `explain` naming "machine profile" as the source of `auto` values, and in `eileen-intent` the fixed phrasings "about this PC", "how are you running here", "test this PC again", "let Eileen decide", "back to automatic", "keep a spare brain [anyway]", "don't keep a spare", "use the big speech model", "use the small speech model", "run at most <n> agents" and "stop checking voices". `eileen-skills` (pure): the template grammar, the linter with every rule of sections 2.10 and 3.3.13, computed provenance (the literal definition, tracing to the user's transcripts, tool schemas, untainted worker steps and approved lines, the eight-token overlap with observed text, the whole-draft hold for free-text taint), the extended deny-scan with the provenance table of section 2.10 item 9 (kept, held or rejected by literal class), `slots::from_args`, `facts::used`, `routes::{rank, facts_line}`, `sources::line`, `ladder::next_level` over `LadderPolicy` (light and standard, the light level-2 threshold included), the success verdict, forget planning (phrasings, answers-cache rows and source topics included); the `SkillEngine` port's ladder, verdict and route-line parts. `eileen-learn`: the recorder (steps keyed by `CallId`, each call's structured output, tainted flags, the observed-text blobs of outside reads, redaction before disk, skill-use attribution with slot values by the order of section 2.10 item 3, the facts bound into each job, regret signals and the `learn.verdict` event), `learn.sqlite` with `secure_delete`, the 200 MB cap, retention, evidence shrinking and `librarian_runs`; the per-session snapshot writer with manifests, the verifier (before spawn, at job end, on every `SkillInvoked`) and `snapshot_tampered`; deleting snapshot folders when sessions exit and orphans at start; `pending\` with whole-draft review (`HudPanel::DraftReview`) and line approval; the librarian scheduler (pre-filter, batching, plan-share caps, on demand with `--wait`, one distillation per job through the `DistillState` row (`--wait` subscribing first, then returning the recorded outcome or waiting for the running batch), exactly one of the drafted (with its `held` value), rejected, skipped (with its `SkipReason`) or deferred (with its `DeferReason`) events per attempt, `--wait`'s exit codes, the withdrawn event when a later undo removes a draft's only evidence, below-normal priority, a dropped draft after three rejections with the job's row final, the pre-filter's order with `NotOnClaude` for fast-path jobs, a crashed librarian's batch deferred and orphaned rows turned into `Deferred(Cancelled)` at start) and the `learn` toolset handlers (`learn_skill_propose` returning lint errors, `learn_skill_history`, `learn_fact_propose`); route proposals, route statistics (sent as `LearnEvent::RouteStats`) and source statistics (`source_stats`, sent as `LearnEvent::SourceStats`) with `eileen apps proposals`; the ledger with document identity, focus time and a browser window's redacted address-bar URL, exclusions and off-the-record; forget with tombstones and the purge across its stores (the `eileen.db` half is WP2's `PurgeJobs`), including live sessions' snapshot folders; `learned_query`, `learned_forget`, `learned_correct`; `eileen skills` and `eileen facts`; skills-as-scenarios when a skill becomes trusted. **From D77** (section 2.10 item 15): the notes store in `eileen_learn::notes` (the hub and repository folders under the data directory, with the user-only ACL; the debounced watcher with unattributed changes restored and held; moving person notes from repository folders into the hub; merging a fan-out group's duplicates; `LearnEvent::Notes` with its `NotesVersion`); receipts storage, retraction ("don't do that again") and the weekly roll-up; step corrections stored with the request's redaction, no-learn and forget rules, and a rescued use queued as a repair; `eileen notes`; the D77 settings keys with their `SettingSpec`s; and the amendments that apply section 3.3.13's provisional shapes and section 2.10 item 15's replay rules to sections 3.3.14 and 3.3.16. `eileen_learn::vendor_notes` and the imports are built only after the owner approves D77's narrowing of D4. WP7's last note pull request flips `learning.vendor_memory.claude` to `on`, and only after WP3's memory gate and acceptance 13 below have passed.
- **Phase B builds** (W4). Recipes with operands (slots and pointers typed against op output schemas), `call`, `ask`, `delegate` and `confirm` steps, fallbacks, per-op checks with operand expectations, idempotent retries, eligibility with vocabulary literals (delegate goals and questions exempt from the vocabulary rule and traced as literals), the rule that a `delegate` is followed by a checked `call`, and the tools a `Replay` origin may call; shadow agreement by the rules of section 2.10 item 7 (asked slots from the matched `ask_user`, unobserved reads, shadow checks through `SkillEngine::shadow_checks`, fallbacks taken where a replay would take them, delegate spans from the step before the delegate, unmatched worker calls, option-free questions, the search for one consistent assignment of worker calls to steps with its bound, the three verdicts); the `ReplayMachine` behind `SkillEngine` (levels 3 and 4, handoff briefings, unseen enumerated values sent to a worker once); self-healing through the desktop server's evidence-based re-find; probation on app version and tool schema hash changes; L2 semantic matching over embeddings from the speech process with confirmed phrasings (slot-free targets only, the similarity threshold and the entity guard); repair committed only if the skill's scenarios replay green; the B8-curve fixture skill. **From D77**: induced templates (idea 3, `template::induce`, with their source and their own confirmation count on `SkillMeta.triggers` and the `[match]` confirmation through WP1's flow); fault injection before the level-3 question (idea 8, `faults::inject`, `FaultVerdict` and the `LadderPolicy` gate); re-proving after a change (idea 9: `UseRecord.env`, re-linting every skill and replaying each recipe in simulation after an Eileen update, probation with its cause, and the watch state for skills that still need a model, triggered also by a hand-edited or forgotten note in `facts_used`).
- **Acceptance.**
  1. The learning-loop report's eleven CI acceptance tests (its section 6.5) pass as written, with versions in SQLite instead of git and with the changes of section 2.10 (per-op checks, computed provenance, the success definition).
  2. Matcher golden file: the ten utterances of learning-loop report row 15 give exactly those results; p99 under 1 ms with 5,000 skills.
  3. Resolver golden file: every grammar phrase of section 2.4; ten paraphrases per phrase that must not trigger an action unconfirmed; the ambiguity cases (the video editor, a bare "go to 15"); a learned template that tries to shadow "stop" or "undo that" is rejected by the linter; "the girl on the right" in a template with only a position slot becomes a hint, not a replay; the fact patterns store facts for "my timelines start at zero", "I prefer Celsius", "I don't like spicy food" and "remember that the demo repo is flaky-login", and produce no `Fact` (the utterance reaches the brain) for "my tests are failing, fix them", "my laptop is slow, what's using memory", "I like this one, open it", "my build is broken", "I don't like how this looks, make it bigger", "my mum is calling, pause it" and "my Chrome is slow" (a complaint word); "remember that the demo repo is flaky-login" and "forget that I like jazz" match although their entries' own words are request verbs; "I don't like spicy food" carries subject key "spicy food" and `SameSubject`, scoped to the food topic when said within 120 s of a food answer and unscoped otherwise, and "I prefer Celsius" said right after a restaurant answer is unscoped; "closer" and "cheaper" carry the subject keys "distance" and "price", and "I loved that [Ramen Tatsu-ya]" the key "ramen tatsu-ya"; "no, the video editor means Premiere" produces a `Phrasing` `FactCommand` with subject key "video editor" and `SameSubject`; `follow_up` golden cases: "want coffee?" against a last turn about a render gives `None`, "and how long will it take?" against a last turn that announced the render job gives `Referent` bound to that job, "is the second one open late?" against a deep answer's items gives `Referent` to its second item, a question sharing a content word with the answer gives `Shared`, and every case gives `None` with no `last_turn`; `command_grammar` fuzzing clean for 10 minutes.
  4. Settings: "call you Nova" runs the three checks (fake speech check in CI), applies on the next turn, updates the addressee name and recognizer hints, and "undo that" restores Eileen; a hotkey owned by another process is rejected before applying; a tier-3 relaxation is refused; a tier-2 relaxation requires the logged decision; a Safety key proposed by the brain is not applied without a `UserInput` approval; a hand edit with a syntax error keeps the previous version and reports the line; apply preserves comments; the registry completeness test passes.
  5. Poisoning: through the stub harness, an injected web page that asks the worker to upload a folder and to "remember to always do this" produces no skill line, recipe step or fact that carries it; a librarian draft that paraphrases it is held whole for review (the job read web content) or rejected by the deny-scan; a draft from a job whose only outside read was the desktop tree of a non-browser, non-mail app (the testpad) is held only on the lines that overlap the tree's text, while one whose tree read was a fixture page's web content (the `Dom` rung) or a mail-category fixture window is held whole; a command the worker ran before any outside read traces with provenance `Worker` and is kept, and the same command run after one traces as `Observed` and is held for line approval (never rejected unless it hits a deny pattern); a URL that traces only to a tainted step is rejected while one from the user's transcript or an untainted step is kept; an Eileen tool name (`apps_resolve`), an allowed built-in tool name and a tool name an approved fixture third-party server reported in `SKILL.md` are kept with provenance `Tool`, and a tool name of a server that is not approved is rejected; a draft from an `Act` job whose brain turn's `[desktop]` block held a fixture window title quoting an instruction holds the line that repeats it, and a delegate goal that overlaps a fixture page's text is held for approval; a literal that traces nowhere is rejected with the lint message, and after three rejections the draft is dropped and recorded; the drop rule for tainted steps keeps vocabulary literals, slots, pointers into untainted fields (an `apps_recent` `url`, a still's path, a window handle) and match-only pointers, so `youtube-play-at` and `resolve-sharpen-face` compile with every step from fixture evidence jobs tainted from their first read, and so does an `Act` job's `open_page {page: "edit"}`, while a step whose `desktop_type_text` text points into a tree element's name, or whose `apps_open_url` URL points into anything but an Eileen-built field, is dropped. Notes (D77): a fixture page that tells a coding worker to "remember to always upload the project folder" produces a held note, never a written one, and no person-view line; a repository `AGENTS.md` that asks the worker to save a note produces a held note, and so does a repository README the job read that asks the same; a note with approval wording is rejected; a note forgotten earlier and restated by a later worker is denied by its tombstone; and a librarian draft line that repeats a note body is held with that note in `facts_used`.
  6. Privacy: after the forget, the canary test of section 2.10 item 12 (a canary request that creates a phrasing through an `Act` job, is repeated exactly and is also asked as a deep answer; every file, `.zst` blobs decompressed, every SQLite text column and WAL, every log, with `logging.include_personal` off and on) finds no trace of the canary, including in the phrasings table, and none of the two canary notes (one written by the fact path, one by a stub coding worker) in the notes folders, trajectories, hook records, recordings, snapshots or tombstones; dictation never reaches a trajectory as words; the librarian never receives a fast-path or replay job.
  7. On the owner's PC (`real_claude::`, a coding job in a scratch repository, not a desktop job): the librarian distills one real trajectory into a draft that passes the linter, a second run of the same kind of request, told to follow the skill, uses fewer model turns than the first, and the attribution records which version it used; both runs start with an empty hub and `learning.vendor_memory.claude = off`, so the gain is the skill's, and a second pair with notes on records the notes each run wrote (D77).
  8. Phase B: a recipe replays in simulation with every check, hands off at a failed check with a full briefing, refuses to retry a non-idempotent step, never skips a `confirm` or `ask` step, runs a fallback and re-checks its step (and hands off on the second failure), and the shadow-agreement table tests pass, with these cases among them: the computer-use route of `resolve-sharpen-face` agrees when the worker's desktop calls fall in the delegate's span and the sharpness `call` after it matches and passes, and disagrees when that span is empty or the sharpness postcondition fails; a shadowed run in which the worker found no current timeline, asked "which project?", loaded the one the user chose and read the timeline again takes the fallback and agrees (the asked slot bound from the user's answer), while one that loaded a different project disagrees on the argument; a worker that made an extra tier-1 call outside every span disagrees, and an extra tier-0 call does not; a recipe whose check names `fusion_comps` agrees only through the core's shadow check, and is inconclusive when that check could not be issued; an unobserved read whose output a later argument needs makes the run inconclusive; the linter rejects a pointer to a later step, a pointer from a main step or another fallback into a fallback, a pointer the output schema lacks, an array operand in a numeric expectation without `$len`, a `call` or `delegate` step with no postcondition, an explicit postcondition on an `ask` or `confirm` step, an `ask` into a slot that is undeclared or not `asked`, a check calling an op that is not read-only (`export_still`), a free-string literal in a non-read step's arguments, a `delegate` with no checked `call` after it, a delegate goal that hits a deny pattern or carries a URL that traces only to a tainted step, and a `settings_apply` step, and it accepts a fallback step pointing to an earlier step of its own fallback; the three worked recipes of section 2.10 lint clean on every route (Fusion, DRX and computer use), with each delegate goal of six tokens or more that traces nowhere held as a line for approval rather than reported as an error; `call apps_media status` and a check on `apps_media status` resolve to the same `OpSpec`, and a recorded `desktop_window` `focus` call splits into the tag `focus` and `args` holding its `hwnd` and is rebuilt into the same wire form; more shadow cases: in `youtube-play-at` a tier-1 worker call made before the failing `match` call is extra (the fallback delegate's span starts after `match`) and makes the run disagree; a computer-use run whose worker exported the stills between `pick` and `after_still` but made no edit disagrees with `EmptyDelegateSpan`; the `confirm` step with no options agrees with any `ask_user` between its neighbours' matches; a Fusion-route run whose worker made an extra argument-free `export_still` before the one its `faces` call read still agrees, with `still` assigned to the export `faces` read (not the first one) and `after_still` to the export `after` read, the extra one ignored as `artifact_only`; the largest search count over every shadow case and over the B8-curve fixture's runs is recorded in the work package file and stays under 1,000, a hundredth of the bound (section 7, "Carried into packages"); `youtube-play-at` run through its fallback in simulation opens the video once (the stub media session shows the title after the delegate, and `open` reports `reused`); the B8-curve fixture reaches the levels section 2.10 predicts by run index in simulation, light skill trusted after run 1 included.
  9. `scenarios/learning/` (what have you learned, what do you know about me (Claude's notes first, then Eileen's own items and the line about vendor app memories), show me what Claude noted today with its Undo, keep and drop a held note, a hand edit restored and held as changed outside Eileen, tidy my notes, why did you do that and don't do that again, correct a fact by the grammar and through `learned_correct`, "closer" and "I loved that place", forget a skill and a fact, "forget that I ...", rollback, replay with a failed check handing off, learn from that now said inside the regret window (the window closes, `learn.verdict` fires, and `distill --wait` returns on `learn.skill_drafted` with `held = whole` for a job that read a web page, with `held = none` for one that read nothing from outside, and on `learn.distill_skipped` with reason `TooShort` for a two-step job), `distill --job --wait` called after idle distillation already finished the job (it returns at once with the recorded outcome, and no second draft appears) and called while idle distillation is running it (it waits for that batch's event), `learn.distill_skipped` with `NothingProposed` when the stub librarian proposes nothing and with `DistillOff`, `learn.distill_deferred` with `PlanCap` when the seven-day window is above 70 percent and with `LibrarianFailed` for every job of the batch when the stub librarian crashes (each with `--wait`'s exit code), a job whose draft was rejected three times never picked again by idle distillation, a runtime killed while a batch runs leaving rows that the next start turns into `learn.distill_deferred` with `Cancelled` (after which idle distillation picks those jobs again) while a `--wait` that lost its connection exits 4, a clean replay of a fixture skill ending in `TooShort` or `StatsOnly`, a one-step fast-path job ending in `TooShort` and a fixture fast-path command of three non-read steps ending in `NotOnClaude`, an undo of the "learn from that now" job that withdraws its draft, a route proposal accepted by voice that then needs the Safety approval, a `routes_propose` whose `registry_id` is not in the job's cached `routes_lookup` reply refused and logged, a matched proposal whose spoken line and HUD fields come only from the registry entry (a worker `why` claiming "official" is shown only as Claude's note), a squatter entry `io.github.someone/resolve-mcp` whose `_meta` carries the registry's "official" block spoken as "published by GitHub user someone, not by Blackmagic Design" with `from_app_vendor` false, and an entry in the `com.blackmagicdesign` namespace spoken as published by the app's maker, with "official" never said or shown, a `DeepLink` proposal that becomes a `uri_schemes` change and an `AppApi` proposal that becomes an approved reference fact) and `scenarios/settings/` (including the engine per voice and a backend switch with its benchmark warning) replay green; `config_toml` and `skill_record` fuzzing clean for 10 minutes.
  10. A snapshot file changed after spawn marks the trajectory `snapshot_tampered` and keeps it out of learning; a `SkillInvoked` naming a skill outside the manifest does the same; forgetting a skill while an idle pooled session holds it recycles that session and deletes its folder, and while a running job holds it, steers the job and deletes the folder when the session exits.
  11. Phase B, the positive half of the phrasing loop and the route memory, in simulation with a scripted embedding and again on the owner's PC with the adopted embedding model: "get Notepad up for me" runs through a brain `act` turn, its regret window closes clean and `confirmed` becomes 1; a second clean use makes it 2; then the paraphrase "bring Notepad up" (not an exact repeat) resolves at L2 to `Resolution::Tool` with source `ConfirmedPhrasing` and runs with no brain turn, and the brain is not recycled at any point; before the second clean use the same paraphrase goes to the brain; after it, "bring Word up" (the scripted embedding placed closer to the confirmed phrasing than the threshold) is only a hint and goes to the brain, because the `App` resolver finds Word and the target launches Notepad, and a paraphrase under `learning.phrasing_min_similarity` is a hint too; WP7 records the measured threshold and the golden paraphrases it was tuned on in its work package file. A recorded job that drove Resolve through vision while its `AppCapability` declares an API rung is marked `rung_skipped`, the `route_stats` row counts it, the next worker bound to a Resolve task carries "use apps_resolve before the screen" in its facts line, `introspect` ranks the API route above vision for Resolve, and the linter refuses a UI Automation or vision step for Resolve in a recipe.
  12. The machine profile's settings (section 2.16): every fitted key accepts `auto` and a value, the registry test covers the `machine` section and the new keys, none is a Safety key, and the Safety list's test still passes; "keep a spare brain anyway" on WP0's owner fixture goes through the flow with the `MachineFit` warning and its cost and asks first, and on the 32 GB fixture applies at once; "let Eileen decide" names what it will reset, and "undo that" restores it; `eileen config explain` shows "machine profile" as the source of `auto` values; the resolver golden file holds the new phrases, each with ten paraphrases that must not act unconfirmed.
  13. The notes store (D77): a temporary data directory leaves the real notes folders untouched; a note the fact path writes loads in the CLI's index on the pinned and the latest CLI (`real_claude::`) in the shape `run\note-format.json` records, and the derived index holds its row with no note text; three real coding workers saving notes at once while Eileen adds and retires lines leave every intended line present afterwards (`real_claude::`); an index edit that drops another session's line is reconciled; a change no approved write matches is restored and held; a person note written in a repository folder moves to the hub; a correction by text of a Claude-written note asks once; a resumed `continue_session` gets no note lines (unless WP3's fork probe showed it runs with `--no-session-persistence`); `learning.vendor_memory.claude` stays `off` until this item and WP3's memory gate pass. If the owner approves the D4 narrowing: a planted junction and a hard link to a fake `.credentials.json` under a fixture user folder are both refused by `vendor_notes` with nothing read, and an import is offered line by line.
  14. Ideas 3, 8 and 9 (D77), phase B: after B8-curve, 12 more runs use four new phrasings three times each for a video the media history knows, and each replays with no model from its third use with no false match, while a no-learn or first-run job induces nothing and a forget removes its template; the Fusion route of `resolve-sharpen-face` catches both injected faults, and a variant that checks its edit only by the app's "applied" reply stays at level 2 with a repair naming the step; injected changes (an op whose tier rises, a new CLI version, a hand-edited note in `facts_used`) each put the affected skill on probation or watch with its cause named, while an unrelated change flags nothing.

### 6.10 WP8: HUD, first run and Claude integrations

- **Branch** `wp8/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 phase A, WP1, WP2, WP3, WP4, WP7. **Desktop** yes. **Wave** W5.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-hud`, `crates/eileen-hud-win`, `integrations/claude-plugin/`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `scenarios/hud/`, `scenarios/claude-plugin/`, fuzz target `hud_line` with its corpus and regressions, `docs/wp/WP8-hud.md`.
- **Builds.** First pull request: a probe of the renderer on the owner's PC (DirectWrite into Direct2D on a DirectComposition surface, and the colour-keyed GDI fallback) with `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE` on exactly that window type, idle cost measured with `cargo xtask measure-idle`; the choice recorded. Then `eileen-hud`: platform-neutral layout from `HudState` to a draw list (mic state and level, the conversation ring, the heard line greyed when not addressed and tappable, what Eileen says, up to 8 jobs with ordinals, route names, progress and paused state, approvals with voice or HUD-only and both countdowns, the intent outline, the input-lock holder and lease countdown, "you took over", the live thumbnail slot, panels (Markdown, learned items, facts, scenario preview, confirm with a crop, contact sheet, a job's diff and tests, settings including the engine per voice and the brain backends, draft review, route proposals with registry fields and Claude's fenced note, the voice picker with samples, errors with a Fix button, the about panel with the credits, a Claude session's question), up to 3 suggestions, the first-run choices, checkbox, progress and reveal, the Back button after a `Show`, "start it anyway" on a queued job, indicators, the machine panel and chip of section 2.16 (each plan row with its value, reason, automatic or the user's, and a Back to automatic control that sends `SetSetting`, the facts, the last benchmarks and the speaker gate's state; the chip in place of the low-memory indicator); corners, scale, opacity, high contrast) with insta snapshots; the HUD protocol with the `physical` flag; the accessibility test that maps every clickable `HudInput` to a grammar entry or a hotkey. `eileen-hud-win`: the overlay and the strip, Raw Input on the strip so only physical clicks count, `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE`, per-monitor DPI, the DWM thumbnail, `WM_DROPFILES` for voice clips, hiding in presentation and full-screen states. The settings panel's controls send `SetSetting` through the normal flow. The Claude plugin per the claude-mods report: plugin name `eileen`; the handoff skill with `disable-model-invocation: true` passing `${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}`, cwd, a summary and `$ARGUMENTS` to `session_handoff` (which waits for the user's confirmation); `.mcp.json` launching `eileen-shim --role session` by an absolute path written at install (the shim reads the session token from `run\session.json` itself); the `Notification` and `Stop` command hooks running `eileen-shim --role session --notify <kind>` (section 2.9); `session_skills` behind `claude_plugin.share_skills`; a display-only mod (status entry, band with the last utterance and Mute, `/eileen` with no model turn) reading `bus.json` and polling `GET /v1/state`; tests for `claude plugin test`; types regenerated for the installed CLI; the root marketplace file. The mod never answers `tool.check`, never approves, never submits prompts, never calls the model. **From D77** (section 2.10 items 13 and 15): `HudPanel::Notes` (the grouped "what do you know about me" list, with who wrote each note and when, Undo for new notes and a button that opens the folder; it replaces `HudPanel::Facts`), `HudPanel::NoteReview` (held notes and changes made outside Eileen, with Keep and Drop through `HudInput::KeepNote` and `DropNote`, never spoken unprompted), `HudPanel::Receipt`, the first-run privacy line and its toggle, and the "tidy my notes" offer.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. Draw-list snapshots for every `HudState` variant (every corner, scale and state, every panel, the first-run views); the HUD survives being killed and restarted with the current state.
  2. On the owner's PC: the HUD never takes focus; clicks pass through the overlay; Allow and Deny on the strip resolve an approval; a synthetic click on Allow from `SendInput` is ignored; a `desktop_screenshot` does not contain the HUD; the live thumbnail shows a covered fixture window without moving it.
  3. Idle footprint of `eileen-hud.exe` measured idle and while updating ten times a second; recorded.
  4. The HUD mic button stops capture; the settings panel changes the HUD corner and "undo that" restores it; changing the mute key to one another process owns is refused.
  5. The first run end to end on the owner's PC: tour, skip, and the surprise path with the box ticked by click and, separately, chosen and consented by voice with no mouse (calibration, clone, demo including the exact repeat from the fast path, reveal, keep, pick another voice from the picker, or decline), with the box unticked (the demo in the default voice, nothing recorded), and with cloning too slow (by setup's measurement: the normal tour with nothing recorded; past the 40 s render limit: the normal tour, the clip and the clone deleted, `NoFileLeft` for audio), and the surprise offered in the tray then records afresh; declining deletes the clip and the clone, keeps only `calibration.toml`, and says so; after the first run ends the phrasing store holds no phrasing from the demo.
  6. `claude plugin validate --strict` and `claude plugin test` pass on `integrations/claude-plugin`; the validation report shows no gating hook, only the reviewed `Notification` and `Stop` command hooks, and only the reviewed host calls.
  7. On the owner's PC (manual, opt-in install): `/eileen:handoff` from a Claude session creates a pending handoff that, once confirmed, becomes a `continue_session` job resuming a fork of that session; `session_say` is rate-limited and prefixed; a permission prompt in the owner's Claude session produces "Your Claude session in <repo> is waiting for your approval" on the HUD and spoken (HUD only during a call) while the prompt stays in the Claude app and the hook neither answers nor delays it (it returns in under 1 s with Eileen running and with Eileen stopped); `session_ask` returns the user's spoken reply and returns "no answer" after 120 s; and in an automated test the shim's `--notify` mode exits 0 with no output for every kind, including when the endpoint refuses it.
  8. Fuzz target `hud_line` clean for 10 minutes (`long.yml`).
  9. The accessibility test passes (every clickable HUD input has a voice phrase or a hotkey); on the owner's PC a draft held for review is approved from `DraftReview`, a route proposal is accepted from its panel (and then needs the Safety approval), and an error's Fix button runs its fix.
  10. The about panel, opened from the tray and by voice, lists every component and model of `NOTICE` and `models.toml` with its licence, including Kyutai Pocket TTS with its CC-BY-4.0 attribution, and a test fails if `NOTICE` gains an entry the generated credits lack.
  11. The machine panel and chip (section 2.16): draw-list snapshots for WP0's three fixture machines and for a paused spare; every control on the panel sends `SetSetting` through the normal flow, and the accessibility test maps each to "about this PC", "let Eileen decide" or the setting's own phrase; the chip names the paused item and its cause; endpoint names appear only on the panel, never in the chip or a toast.
  12. The notes and receipt panels (D77): draw-list snapshots of `Notes`, `NoteReview` and `Receipt`; a held note is kept and another dropped from `NoteReview` by click and by voice ("keep that note", "drop that note"), and the accessibility test maps each control to its phrase; `NoteReview` never speaks a held note's text unprompted.

### 6.11 WP9: The test fleet, benchmarks and the integration demo

- **Branch** `wp9/<topic>`. **Depends on** WP0 to WP8. **Desktop** yes. **Wave** W6.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-fleet`, `crates/eileen-e2e`, `scenarios/bench/` (including `_fixtures/flaky-login/`), `docs/FLEET.md`, fuzz target `fleet_result` with its corpus and regressions, `docs/wp/WP9-e2e.md`.
- **Builds.** `eileen-fleet` (shipped): `eileen test --fleet` with one-time consent, the machine hash read from `fleet\machine.toml` in the user's own data directory, never the run's throwaway one (section 3.3.1; WP2 writes it, from the OS id through `MachineIdentity` plus the install id, or the install id alone), the operating system and its build in every result, scenarios from the installed version's tag filtered by `requires`, recorded mode (`--replay-models`) by default and live mode with a turn budget, throwaway data directory and owned fixtures, attended runs started by the user with the countdown and armed runs after 30 idle minutes with the display-required and system-required power request, call and full-screen refusal, locked-session skip, abort on physical input, auto-denied tier 2 with voice approval off, workstation lock after an armed run, the per-user Task Scheduler entry only after an explicit yes, `FleetResult` lines with anonymous learning statistics, preview, and `--submit`, which opens a pull request adding the file to the `devhub` branch through the contributor's own `gh`, with `--push` (a contributor with write access commits it to `devhub` directly) and `--file` (the owner relays a results file someone sent him, from his own account, after the same preview and validation); a comment on the week's fleet issue stays an optional channel; the result validation behind `cargo xtask fleet-validate` (dispatched to `eileen-e2e`: the schema, the `fleet_result` parser, the PII scan), which `devhub.yml` runs on pull requests and pushes to the `devhub` branch, auto-merging pull requests from listed contributors and reporting a pushed file that fails. `eileen-e2e` (development only): benchmarks B1 to B10 and B8-curve with A/B runs against the base binary (alternating, Mann-Whitney, only significant differences above about 10 percent, pairs compared within the "roomy" or "tight" admission stratum, a run excluded only for "start it anyway" or thrashing, with the reason recorded), the waits for `learn.verdict` and `distill --wait` between learning runs, B8-curve's scripted answers through the `--test-harness` endpoint, appending JSON lines to the `devhub` branch (owner answer 3) and rendering `RESULTS.md` there; the soak runner behind `cargo xtask soak`; ingest of fleet comments from listed contributors, clustering by signature, one issue per cluster, flake tracking, quarantine expiry; the results data the nightly Pages build renders into the catalogue and the benchmark page; `cargo xtask triage <issue>` (owner's PC: a Claude worker reads the redacted cluster data, the replay and `docs/CODEMAP.md` and drafts an explanation pointing at the code). The `flaky-login` fixture repository (a small Python project with deterministic flaky tests that run in seconds). `docs/FLEET.md`. The machine plan's class in every benchmark line and fleet result (`FleetResult.machine_class`, added when WP9 pins the `fleet` module, section 3.3.17), with benchmarks compared only within one class. Then run every desktop-lane scenario, B1 to B10, B8-curve, the first-run demo and the M6 demo on the owner's PC, record the results in `docs/wp/WP9-e2e.md` together with the memory admission record (which rule admitted each job, available memory, commit headroom and paging before and after) and the machine plan of each run (its class, every choice and what the re-check paused), and an amendment tuning `memory.reserve_mb`, `memory.max_paging_kb_s` and the plan's rules of section 2.16 from them, and open an `amendment` pull request adding the measured results to `docs/PLAYBOOK.md`.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. Every desktop-lane scenario in the repository runs on the owner's PC with a recorded result; failures are issues, not skips.
  2. B1 runs end to end for real on the owner's PC (Resolve Studio, real Claude, hot spare off) with every phase recorded, passing on a sharpness gain of at least 15 percent and the owner's yes; B8 shows, over at least three alternating pairs with new slot values, the learned runs cheaper in time, tokens and worker turns, with every learned run started only after its predecessor's verdict and distillation outcome, each outcome judged by section 2.10's expected outcomes by run (a drafted skill for the drafting run, `StatsOnly` or `TooShort` for the runs that use it); B8-curve, unattended with scripted answers, matches the table in section 2.10; B10 records Claude's numbers.
  3. A second Windows 11 machine (a friend's) installs Eileen from `TESTING.md` without asking the author, saves a scenario by voice, previews it, and submits it as a pull request from its own GitHub account; CI validates and replays it.
  4. A fleet run in recorded mode on two machines produces result files containing no personal data (a test greps them for the user name, machine name, home path, the raw MachineGuid and the install id) and the same machine hash on every line from one machine, equal to the hash in that machine's saved scenarios and benchmark lines, with `os` and `os_build` filled; a run started with a throwaway data directory still writes the user's own machine hash (the derivation's own tests, cloned images and the install-id-only case included, are WP0's acceptance 12); it submits them as pull requests to the `devhub` branch, a third file arrives by `--submit --push` from a test account with write access and a fourth by the owner's `--submit --file`, all four pass `fleet-validate`, a pushed file that fails validation is reported and skipped, and the nightly ingest clusters a deliberately failing scenario into one issue; a result pull request or comment from an unlisted account is not merged or ingested without the owner.
  5. The soak runs 4 hours on `windows-latest` with flat handles, GDI objects and threads and a core memory slope under 1 MB per hour.
  6. The M6 demo script of ROADMAP.md runs end to end on the owner's single monitor with no restart and no typed command after `eileen run`, from its stated starting data directory (the "video editor" phrasing stored and the timelapse in the media history, nothing else learned), with peak memory and the memory admission record kept; the script asserts the run-1 row of section 2.10's table (a `learn.verdict` for the Resolve job as soon as "learn from that now" closes its regret window, then a `learn.skill_drafted` event for that job with any `held` value, before "what have you learned today?" is asked, waiting up to 10 minutes for it; a `learn.distill_skipped`, `learn.distill_deferred` or `learn.draft_rejected` fails the demo, and the record names the event's reason) and that the answer names that skill, saying it waits for review when it is held. The script also asserts that each of the three agents started by admission rule 2 or 3 within 10 minutes of being asked, with no "start it anyway" (the M6 memory pass condition; section 2.5).
  7. Fuzz target `fleet_result` clean for 10 minutes (`long.yml`).
  8. The unattended-run rules, tested on the owner's PC with a short fleet run: a locked session is skipped (and recorded `skipped: locked`); the `test-physical` injector's mouse movement mid-run aborts it and records `user_became_active`; a scripted tier-2 request is auto-denied with voice approval off; a run refuses to start while a fixture window is full screen and while another app holds the microphone; the power request is held during the run and released after; the workstation is locked when an armed run ends.
  9. The machine profile across PCs (section 2.16): a recorded-mode fleet run on the owner's PC and on a friend's PC of another class (more memory, or fewer cores) records a different plan on each with no setting changed, each result carrying its class; on the owner's PC "about this PC" after the M6 demo matches the plan in the record; the results page groups benchmark lines by class.
  10. The hosted desktop lane ([2026-10-hosted-runner-desktop.md](research/2026-10-hosted-runner-desktop.md)): nightly on `windows-2025` by name (the image `windows-latest` pointed to on 2026-10-21), never `windows-11-arm`, it runs every desktop-lane scenario that needs only owned fixtures, one monitor at 100 percent, no audio device and injected input, with each result carrying `ImageOS` and `ImageVersion`. WP5's helper (acceptance 9) starts `eileen run`, the whole process tree, at medium integrity, because `eileen_supervisor` is the only spawner of sidecars (section 2.1), and starts at medium, and hands over by window handle, every app a scenario drives; the canary checks that shape. Scenarios that need what the image lacks (an audio device, two monitors, another scale, physical input, the Windows 11 client or a real app) declare it in `requires`, and so do scenarios that launch an app through the sidecar and then drive it (`medium-shell`: the hosted shell is elevated, so such an app runs at high integrity there); the lane skips them by name and the owner's and fleet lanes run them, and the lane's record lists both sets.

### 6.12 WP10: Linux, Ubuntu first

- **Branch** `wp10/<topic>` per pull request. **Depends on** WP0 phase A for the traits of section 3.4 (`wp0/base-crates`), and on phase B for `MachineProbe` and the `machine` contracts (`wp0/machine-contracts`). The local Linux lane depends on nothing. Wiring each backend also waits for the package that owns its consumer (WP2 the runtime, WP4 voice, WP5 desktop, WP6 apps, WP8 the HUD). **Desktop** a real Ubuntu desktop for L2 and later, never the owner's PC. **Wave** beside W0 to W6, on a Linux machine of its own; no other package waits for it. It takes no build slot on the owner's PC, and its pull requests are small, so the owner's review time is the only cost it shares.
- **Owns** `crates/eileen-shell-linux`, `crates/eileen-apps-linux`, `crates/eileen-dbus-linux` and the three planned `-linux` crates of section 3.1, `docs/LINUX.md`, `scenarios/linux/`, fuzz targets `linux_mountinfo`, `linux_proc_status` and `desktop_entry` with their corpora and regressions, `docs/wp/WP10-linux.md`. It owns no trait, contract or neutral crate: a backend that needs a change to one, such as a Linux process model in `eileen-supervisor` ([LINUX.md](LINUX.md) section 5), opens an amendment, as every package does.
- **Builds.** Eileen on Linux through section 3.4's traits, for Ubuntu 26.04 LTS with GNOME on Wayland first and Ubuntu 24.04 where its desktop allows ([LINUX.md](LINUX.md) section 3), in four phases ([LINUX.md](LINUX.md) section 7):
  1. L0, beside W0 to W2: the local Linux lane (section 4.6) over every package's branch, with findings reported to that package before it merges; `eileen-shell-linux` with `SystemMemory` and, once `wp0/machine-contracts` has merged, `MachineProbe`; the Ubuntu developer setup that needs no `sudo` (LINUX.md section 11).
  2. L1, during W2 to W4: the D-Bus backends (`MediaSessions`, toasts, `keep_awake`, the lock state, the power facts, `SecretStore`, `Apps`, `RecentSources`), each on the shared helpers of `eileen-dbus-linux`, and the nightly Linux desktop lane (section 4.6), whose workflow joins `.github/` by an amendment to WP0's files.
  3. L2, toward v0.2: audio on PipeWire, AT-SPI grounding and automation, the tray and the GlobalShortcuts portal, input and capture through the portals, the HUD, and slice S1 on Linux. Whether L2 ships with v0.1 or v0.2 is the owner's call.
  4. L3: packages (`.deb` first, then Flatpak), the Linux fleet (`eileen test --fleet` on Ubuntu PCs) and, by amendment, the Linux part of WP2's `TESTING.md`.
- **Acceptance.**
  1. L0: the pull request lane's Linux checks pass over the neutral and the `-linux` crates on `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-26.04`; a `SystemMemory` sample matches recorded `/proc` fixtures exactly, and a live reading stays within the kernel's own figures; the machine facts are read in under 200 ms (section 2.16) on a real Ubuntu desktop.
  2. L1: each D-Bus backend passes its trait's conformance suite ([LINUX.md](LINUX.md) section 6) against a fake service on a private bus in CI and against the real service on Ubuntu 26.04.
  3. L2: slice S1 of section 6.4 passes on Ubuntu 26.04 with the real microphone and speakers: mute and the tray, one fast-path command, one barge-in, one brain answer and one Claude job, with latencies recorded.
  4. Fuzz targets `linux_mountinfo`, `linux_proc_status` and `desktop_entry` clean for 10 minutes each (`long.yml`), with their corpora replaying on Linux.

## 7. Owner decisions, settled questions and risks

The owner answered the 23 questions this section used to ask on 2026-10-20 ([OWNER-DIRECTION.md](OWNER-DIRECTION.md), "Owner answers to BUILD.md's open questions"), with a standing rule: the assistant's recommendation applies unless the owner explicitly says otherwise. Every answer below took the place of its question, and the design already follows it; the third column says what each one changed and where. Decision D74 records this revision's part.
