- Section 7's table now says, for each of the 23 answers, what it changed and where, in place of the questions; the four questions the first round's revision had added are settled by the standing rule as recommended (they were already in D54, D56, D58 and D63); the owner's entries after the answers are listed beside it; the section is renamed "Owner decisions, settled questions and risks", and the reviewers' note in the header says no question is left open for the owner.
- `docs/DECISIONS.md`: the twenty statuses among D17 to D38 that read only "accepted" now name owner answer 1, following the file's own precedent for an owner's acceptance; D21, D25, D39 to D50 and the records added since already named theirs; D53, D58 and D60 say they are amended by D74. History is otherwise unchanged.
- The scrub of owner answer 15 is done rather than scheduled: `docs/RESEARCH.md` (its opening line and section 8) and `docs/NAMING.md` describe the earlier working names and the franchise without naming them, as D15 and D16 already did; `wp0/guidance` no longer rewrites them, phase B's exempt list and check 6 say they were scrubbed, and WP0 acceptance 8 proves the seeded denylist against the pre-scrub copies of all three files from git history and against the current files (4b.3, 6.2).

**Fleet machine identity on every OS (owner answer 6).**

- The derivation is one function in the platform layer, `eileen_platform::machine::machine_hash`, with `normalise_os_id` (Windows' GUID form and Linux's plain form follow one rule; an id that is not 32 hex digits, all zeros or `uninitialized` counts as none) and length-prefixed inputs; `FileMachineIdentity` reads Linux's machine-id files and is tested on Linux from v0.1; `InstallId` is a contract type, and the install id never leaves the PC, so the public salt does not let anyone recompute a hash from an OS id (3.3.1, 3.4; WP0 phase A and acceptance 12).
- The hash used to be computed only by WP9's fleet runner in W6, while scenario save (WP2) and voice consent records (WP4) needed it from W1 and W2. The runtime now creates the install id at `eileen setup` or the first run and writes the hash to `fleet\machine.toml` at every start; scenario save, consent records, the fleet runner (reading the user's own data directory, never the run's throwaway one) and benchmark lines all read it there, so one value appears in every result and scenario from that install (2.8, 2.15, 3.3.14, 3.5, 4.2 step 5, 4.7, 4.8; WP2 builds and acceptance 8 and 13, WP9 builds and acceptance 4). `eileen-runtime` may depend on `eileen-platform`, which its memory sampler already needed (3.1).
- `FleetResult` names its operating system (`os: Platform`, `os_build` in place of `windows_build`), and failure clusters use the OS build (3.3.16, 4.8).
- Results arrive as a contributor's pull request, a contributor's own commit (`--submit --push`, for those with write access) or the owner's relay (`--submit --file`); `devhub.yml` validates pull requests and pushes alike and reports a pushed file that fails, which ingest skips (3.5, 4b.3, 4.8; WP9 builds and acceptance 4).

**The other answers and entries.**

- Resolve (answer 9): `doctor resolve --route mcp|bridge` runs the checks through one route, and WP6 acceptance 1 runs them through ResolveMCP and again through the bridge once the owner is on 21.1 or later, so the fuscript Lua bridge stays a tested fallback (2.7, 3.5, 4.2, WP6 builds and acceptance 1).
- The nightly lane (answer 10) is unattended from its first night, with no attended trial and no per-night arming; `cargo xtask nightly-local --install` creates the Task Scheduler entry once (4.6, 4b.5).
- Languages (answer 21): `LanguageSupport` is a contract, the brain backend and the worker driver report one through `languages()` as the speech stages already did, each from the component itself, and section 2.8 states the later version's fallback (another engine that supports the language, or typing); Eileen's code holds no list of languages (section 1's proposed-ideas table, 2.8, 3.3.8; WP3 acceptance 6, WP4 builds).
- ML projects (the owner's Colab entry): `JobKind::MlProject` is reserved with `JobKind::built_in_v0_1`, and the dispatcher refuses it with a `JobKindUnavailable` error that offers a research job; the connector stays v0.2 in section 1's spine and in ROADMAP, with its route chosen at v0.2 design time as the entry's route note says (section 1, 2.5, 3.3.2, 3.3.9, section 7; WP1 builds and acceptance 14).

- 2026-10-21, amendment from `wp0/base-crates`, where the build guard of section 6.1 rule 1 was built. Windows file locks are mandatory, so a holder record written inside a locked file could not be read by the next command or by `cargo xtask slots`; the record sits beside its lock as `<name>.holder`, and `--kill-stale` removes the records whose locks are free. The samples interface gains `slot_held_now()`, because the runtime must mark `slot_held` on every sample and paging window it writes and section 3.1 lets it use only the `samples` module. Slot 1's rule is stated as the rule for any second build, whichever slot index is free. No contract changes.

Section 7 moves the question to the settled items, narrows the open risk to the image staying as measured and notes the injected half of the Raw Input signal. Section 4.1's rule for `syn` said the newest 2.x release at least 14 days old; it now says the newest 3.x release at least 14 days old. 3.0.6 (2026-09-16) satisfied the cooling-off period when Dependabot proposed it on 2026-10-21. No workspace member uses `syn` yet (only `tools/repo-tools`, from WP0 phase B, will), so nothing that builds today changes; the comment beside the pin in `Cargo.toml` follows in a `deps` pull request. No decision record and no contract changes.
- 2026-10-25, amendment applying the owner's direction of 2026-10-22 ([OWNER-DIRECTION.md](OWNER-DIRECTION.md#2026-10-22)): learn the person through the models' own memory and use every native advantage of the tools Eileen routes to, and adopt Ayham's ten learning ideas (pull request #34) with ideas 1, 3, 4, 8 and 9 in v0.1 and the rest after v0.1. Decision D77 records it, with the research in [2026-10-learning-the-person.md](research/2026-10-learning-the-person.md). Section 0's summary paragraph and reviewers' line say so. The person moves from `learn.sqlite` into the claude CLI's own notes, kept in Eileen's data directory (a hub per user, a folder per repository), with the fact rows as a derived index holding no note text (section 2.10 item 15, new). The brain reads the hub through `--append-system-prompt-file` and a `[notes]` block that replaces `[facts]` (2.3); the grammar gains the receipt and notes phrases (2.4), and the user control list names them (2.10 item 10); Claude's notes do not load into the owner's own Claude sessions in v0.1 (2.9); coding sessions write notes natively only after WP3's memory gate, WP7's note acceptance and the `--restricted` question allow it, the web and screen workers and the pool get note lines, and `continue_session` (whose transcript persists) and the librarian get none (2.5, 2.10 item 15); a `Notes` path class, held writes, a watcher that restores and holds unexplained changes, tombstones of keyed hashes and a wider forget plan guard them (2.10 items 9, 11 and 12, 2.12); receipts and step corrections join capture and measurement (2.10 items 1, 2, 6 and 13), fault injection and re-proving join the ladder (item 5), and induced templates join distillation (item 2); file checkpointing is off, workers get `--fallback-model`, and repository instruction files reach coding jobs through `--add-dir` (2.5); the new keys are Safety keys (2.11); the data directory gains `memory\` and `learning\pending\notes\` (2.15); 3.3.8's mapping table gains a Memory row, and 3.3.13 gains provisional shapes (including `ToolSource`'s phrasing id, the induced templates' `[match]` routing, the HUD's notes panels and inputs, `learned_note_propose` and the new settings keys) that WP7 applies by amendment with WP0, WP1, WP3 and WP8; 3.5 gains `eileen notes`; section 1 gains the receipt and notes rows in E11 and a second proposed-ideas table; WP1's builds and acceptance 17, WP3's builds and acceptance 4 and 8, WP7's builds and acceptance 5, 6, 7, 9, 13 and 14, and WP8's builds and acceptance 12 carry the work; section 7 records the entry, three questions waiting for the owner (D4's `~/.claude` rule, D40's `--restricted` fence, and how far v0.1 uses Claude's own note-taking), the local-first open point with its recommendation applied, two questions carried into packages and six risks; ARCHITECTURE.md's state, crate and learning lines follow. The design applies every finding of the critics' review of the first draft, some in an equivalent form the pull request names; none was declined. A review of the pull request then gave `continue_session` no note lines in v0.1, limited the `Notes` class to the folders a session's `MemorySpec` names, kept memory folders out of `--add-dir`, restored the `Stale` rule for vocabulary facts and gave note-backed facts a `Stale` retirement that leaves the loaded index, routed an unconfirmed induced template through `[match]` at any level, widened the note-overlap check to every file text a job read, recorded the `Notes` class against D40, and corrected the research note's account of Claude's app memory; every finding was applied. No Rust changes.
- 2026-10-25, amendment from `docs/platform-file-deadlines`, answering Ayham's question from WP10's drafts: do section 3.4's synchronous methods that read files take a deadline, or does each implementation bound its reads on a helper thread? Both. Section 2.1 rule 2 gives every call to another process a deadline, and a file read is one when a server holds the file (a folder redirected to a share, a home folder on an NFS or FUSE mount). Section 3.4's opening now says that such a method takes the caller's `Deadline`, as `AppHost::call`, `Grounding` and `Automation` do, and that its implementation enforces it on a helper thread, because a hung read cannot be interrupted. `RecentSources::recent` (the Recent folder in the roaming profile, `recently-used.xbel` under the home folder), `RecentSources::thumbnail` (the file, or the thumbnail cache) and `ShellHost::set_run_at_sign_in` (on Linux, the XDG autostart entry under the home folder) take one. A check of every other method in 3.4 adds five: `Apps::index` reads the Start menu's shortcuts, which Folder Redirection can move to a server, and on Linux the desktop entries under the home folder; `Apps::launch` starts a desktop app from a shortcut that may sit there, and on Linux may read the entries again to find the app; `Apps::open_path` opens the user's own file, which on Linux it opens itself to hand the portal a descriptor (WP10's `Opener::open_path` already bounds that by a deadline); `RecentSources::watch` opens each root it watches, user folders that a redirect or a mount may hold, and its deadline bounds that setup, not the subscription; `MachineProbe::facts` measures the data folder's volume, which on Linux sits in the home folder. The others need none: `AppHost::call`, `Apps::main_window` and the UI Automation calls of `Grounding` and `Automation` already take one, and the rest read no file of the user's (`MachineIdentity` reads system files, `SecretStore` calls the OS's credential service, `Apps::open_url` and `open_uri` hand an address to the shell or the portal, the memory and machine readings besides the data folder's volume come from the kernel, and the audio, volume, input, capture, text insertion, media and HUD traits touch no file). Section 3.1 lets `eileen-platform`'s fakes use `eileen-stdx`, on whose `ManualClock` they keep simulated time. No decision record and no contract changes.
- 2026-10-25, amendment from `wp10/amend-spawn-bans`. The spawn bans named only the free functions `tokio::spawn`, `tokio::task::spawn` and `tokio::task::spawn_blocking`, so a task started through a method passed clippy unnamed, outside its span and unregistered, against section 2.13 and D34. The block section 4b.2 quotes, the root `clippy.toml` and `xtask/clippy.toml` now also ban `tokio::runtime::Handle::spawn`, `tokio::runtime::Runtime::spawn` and zbus's `Executor::spawn` (a bare `tokio::spawn` under zbus's `tokio` feature); await-tree's `spawn`, `spawn_anonymous` and `spawn_derived_root`, both the free functions and `Registry`'s methods, which call `tokio::spawn` inside await-tree without the caller's span or the panic log (`eileen_rt::task::registry()` hands the registry to every crate that uses `eileen-rt`); and `tokio::task::JoinSet` as a type, because its `collect` and `extend` start tasks inside tokio as its spawn methods do, where no method ban reaches. Every reason points to `eileen_rt::task`, and those a `-linux` crate can meet also name the way there, because those crates may not use `eileen-rt` (section 3.1): a local task on the bridge's `LocalSet` (`tokio::task::spawn_local` inside a job), and, for the blocking work a `JoinSet` can also spawn, a thread of its own (`eileen_stdx::thread::spawn_named`), since blocking work inside a job stalls the bridge's one thread. The zbus and await-tree entries have no `allow-invalid`: clippy says nothing of a path whose crate a build does not load, zbus defines `Executor::spawn` in every build, and the workspace builds await-tree with its `tokio` feature (section 4.1), so a path that stops resolving after a version bump shows as a warning in the clippy log instead of dropping its ban without a word. Nothing on main calls any of them. The blocking-pool forms, `tokio::runtime::Handle::spawn_blocking` and `tokio::runtime::Runtime::spawn_blocking`, join by a later amendment once #65 and #66 have merged: `eileen-apps-linux` calls them on purpose, in tests that hold a runtime's blocking pool and, with #66, in its file reads, and that crate is not edited while those two are open. `tokio::task::spawn_local` and `LocalSet::spawn_local` stay allowed, the `-linux` crates' way onto the bridge's `LocalSet`; std's scoped threads are left for a later amendment too, since their one caller on main is a test in `eileen-shell-linux`. No decision record and no contract changes.
- 2026-10-25, amendment from `wp0/stubs`, whose first lock of the stubs' native dependencies (section 3.1) met two things the plan had not. First, `sherpa-onnx-sys` 1.13.8, pinned exactly by section 4.1, takes `zip` 2.6.1 as a build dependency, and three advisories against it (RUSTSEC-2026-0367, RUSTSEC-2026-0368 and RUSTSEC-2026-0369, published 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10) list only `zip` 9.0.0 as patched, which no 1.x release of `sherpa-onnx-sys` accepts; its 2.0.0 (2026-10-23) is a major release inside the cooling-off period. The build script calls `zip` only for iOS `.xcframework.zip` archives, unpacks Windows' `.tar.bz2` with `tar` and `bzip2`, and links none of it into a binary, so `deny.toml` ignores the three advisories with their reasons until the pin can move (decision D78, section 4b.3, WP0 acceptance 3). Second, the security-patch rule of section 4.1 applied to three crates entering the lock for the first time through the same build script's download client: `rustls` 0.23.46 (2026-10-14; RUSTSEC-2026-0346 to RUSTSEC-2026-0349), `rustls-webpki` 0.103.16 (2026-10-20; RUSTSEC-2026-0382) and `webpki-roots` 1.0.10 (2026-10-19; RUSTSEC-2026-0381), each the oldest release its advisories list as patched. Every other crate the stubs add to the lock is at least 14 days old (each newer release cargo chose was taken back with `cargo update --precise`), except section 4.1's own pins `keyring-core` 1.0.1 and `global-hotkey` 0.9.0, the verified starting set.
- 2026-10-25, amendment keeping later connectors, routines and devices possible, at the owner's request that the architecture support capabilities not planned yet without hard-coding the apps involved, and the owner's approval of the recommendations with one addition, a speaker condition (decision D79). Section 2.14 gains five provisional reservations (a general connector input with `Observed` events and manifests that wrap an approved MCP server; data classes as open ids on manifests, ops and taint; paired devices with per-device opt-in and room for a device-signed approval; a reply `sink` beside the speech frames' `source`; routines with standing authorization and a budget lane per job), and states that a draft from an unattended job is a finished artifact, not an open approval, and that a model trained on the user's data is disabled when its data is forgotten. Section 2.12 gains the disclosure rule by data class, deciding by the audience state and a private screen, with an optional speaker condition that takes effect only while the speaker gate is `on` after D76's bar. The code blocks of sections 3.3.2, 3.3.3, 3.3.7, 3.3.12 and 3.3.14 mark the reservations and the names to make generic as comments; the pinned modules change only by a later code amendment of their owning package. Generic wording replaces product names in 2.14's connector paragraph (decisions D11 and D37 keep them) and the video site's name in the URL rule; the registry host stays the one host in v0.1. Nothing in v0.1's build or acceptance changes.
- 2026-10-25, amendment from `wp10/linux-desktop-lane`, which builds the Linux desktop lane that section 4.6 planned from WP10's phase L1 ([LINUX.md](LINUX.md) section 6). `.github/workflows/linux-desktop.yml` runs on `ubuntu-26.04`, which is generally available, nightly, on demand and on pull requests that change the lane or a `-linux` crate's `tests/session/`, so its own pull requests show it running. `.github/linux-desktop/session.sh` makes the headless desktop session: a logind session of the runner's user on seat0, entered through `systemd-run` with PAM's `login` stack and made active, so logind and polkit treat the tests as a local user's, with the session bus and services that section 4.6's row lists. notification-daemon has an Xvfb display of its own, since it crashes on Wayland and weston's headless backend offers no seat. Section 4.7 gains the gate for tests that need such a session (`linux_session::` modules, a `-linux` crate's second test binary at `tests/session/main.rs`, and a `linux-session` nextest profile that only the lane runs) and its layer row, and says the tests are for the lane's session alone, since on a developer's own desktop the one that asks logind to lock the session can lock the screen; section 4b.3 gains the profile, the workflow and check 16's third module. The `default` and `ci` filters now also match such a module at the top of a test binary, which `owner_pc::` and `real_claude::` gain too. PLAYBOOK.md 5.10 and ARCHITECTURE.md's testing paragraph follow. The first four session tests check `ShellHost` against logind and the notification server. No contract or decision record changes.
- 2026-10-26, amendment from `wp10/deny-zstd-advisories`. Four RustSec advisories dated 2026-10-16, whose ids the database assigned on 2026-10-26, affect crates that `zip` 2.6.1 (D78's build dependency of `sherpa-onnx-sys` 1.13.8) takes, so `cargo deny --locked check` failed its advisories step on `main` and on every pull request from 2026-10-26: RUSTSEC-2026-0391 against `zstd` 0.13.3 (`stream::raw` `with_context` constructors; patched in 0.14.3), RUSTSEC-2026-0398 and RUSTSEC-2026-0403 against `zstd-safe` 7.3.0 (the `seekable` feature, and a failed legacy frame through `stream::write::Decoder`; patched in 8.2.0) and RUSTSEC-2026-0410 against `zstd-sys` 2.1.1 (wasm32 only; patched in 2.1.2). No patch is taken: `zip` 2.6.1's `zstd = "0.13"` excludes the patched `zstd` and `zstd-safe`, and none of the four reaches code an Eileen build runs (`zip` never calls `with_context`, enables neither `seekable` nor `legacy`, and decodes only through `stream::read::Decoder`; the graph targets are not wasm32). `deny.toml` ignores the four with their reasons and bans `zstd` below 0.14.3 outside `zip`, `zstd-safe` outside `zstd` and `zstd-sys` outside `zstd-safe`, so the ignores cover nothing else, until the lock takes the patched releases (decision D80, amending D78's "and nothing else"; section 4b.3). No contract changes.
